A_{2}:=\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\text{Id}-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}\right)-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Bigl\|\int H^{m_{t}^{\prime}}_{\pi^{t,x}}\otimes H^{m_{t}}_{\pi^{t,x}}d\pi^{t,x}\Bigr\|_{\text{op}}.

First, by the proof of Theorem
1
we have that

A_{1}\leq\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Biggl(C\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr)\Bigl(L_{t}+\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}(K_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\beta})^{\xi}\Bigr)+\sigma_{t}|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|A_{t}\Biggr),

so we only need to show the bound on the
A_{2}
.
Now, fix
t
such that
\sigma_{t}>0
and let
\pi^{t,x}
be the posterior law of
Z
given
X_{t}=x
.
We have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\text{Id}-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}\right)

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\|\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}-\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\|_{\text{op}}+\lambda_{\min}\left(-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}.

By Proposition
11

\|\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}-\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\|_{\text{op}}\leq CK_{t}^{\xi}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-(2+\beta\xi)/2}\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr),

so

\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\|\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}-\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}(K_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\beta})^{\xi}\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr).

Moreover, since
\alpha_{t}\text{Id}\preceq\nabla^{2}u_{t}\preceq A_{t}\text{Id}
, we have

•

if
\sigma_{t}^{\prime}\geq 0
, using Brascamp-Lieb inequality

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\lambda_{\max}\left(\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\prime}\alpha_{t}}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}},

•

if
\sigma_{t}^{\prime}<0
, using Cramer-Rao inequality

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\lambda_{\min}\left(\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{\nu^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}d\nu^{t,x}\right)+\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\prime}A_{t}}{1+A_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

Combining the last three displays yields

\lambda_{\min}\left(\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\text{Id}-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}^{3}}\int(H_{\text{Id}}^{p^{t,x}})^{\otimes 2}dp^{t,x}\right)\geq\frac{-1}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Biggl(C\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr)\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}(K_{t}\sigma_{t}^{\beta})^{\xi}+\sigma_{t}|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|A_{t}\Biggr).

(80)

Let us now control the mixed covariance term.
Set

\Gamma_{t}(x):=\int H^{m_{t}^{\prime}}_{\pi^{t,x}}(z)\otimes H^{m_{t}}_{\pi^{t,x}}(z)d\pi^{t,x}(z).

For unit vectors
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, Cauchy-Schwarz gives

|h^{\top}\Gamma_{t}(x)h^{\prime}|\leq\sqrt{\text{Var}_{\pi^{t,x}}\bigl(\langle h,m_{t}^{\prime}(Z)\rangle\bigr)}\sqrt{\text{Var}_{\pi^{t,x}}\bigl(\langle h^{\prime},m_{t}(Z)\rangle\bigr)}.

By Assumption
5
,

\text{Var}_{\pi^{t,x}}(\langle h,m_{t}^{\prime}(Z)\rangle)\leq L_{t}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}/(1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2})
.
On the other hand, the same argument as in the proof of Corollary
4
(Brascamp–Lieb on
\nu^{t,x}

plus Proposition
11
with
\xi=0
) yields

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\text{Var}_{\pi^{t,x}}\bigl(\langle h^{\prime},m_{t}(Z)\rangle\bigr)\leq C\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr).

Taking suprema over
h,h^{\prime}
gives

\|\Gamma_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq CL_{t}\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr),

hence

\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\|\Gamma_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}L_{t}\exp\Bigl(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Bigr).

(81)

Finally, combining (
80
) and (
81
) gives the lower bound on
A_{2}
.
∎

B.5.3
Proof of Corollary
5

Proof.

By Corollary
4
, for every
t
such that
\sigma_{t}>0
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}+\frac{C_{0}}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\exp\Big(CK_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Big)\Big(\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}+L_{t}\Big).

(82)

Under the Lipman reduced model,
(m_{t})_{\#}\pi
is
(\alpha_{t},\beta,K_{t})
-weakly log-concave with

\alpha_{t}=\frac{\alpha}{f_{t}^{2}},\qquad K_{t}=\frac{K}{f_{t}^{\beta}},\qquad L_{t}=\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}|}{f_{t}}.

Moreover, since
\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2}\geq\alpha_{t}
, we have for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
,

K_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\leq K_{t}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{-\beta}=\frac{K^{2}}{f_{t}^{2\beta}}\Big(\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\alpha}\Big)^{\beta}=K^{2}\alpha^{-\beta},

hence the exponential factor in (
82
) is uniformly bounded on
(0,1)
. Furthermore, by Assumption
1
we have that there exists
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that
\gamma\leq\sigma_{\frac{1}{2}}\wedge f_{\frac{1}{2}}
and
f
is increasing and
\sigma
is decreasing so

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}L_{t}=\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}|f_{t}}{f_{t}^{2}+\alpha\sigma_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}|f_{t}}{\gamma^{2}\wedge(\alpha\gamma^{2})}\leq Cf_{t}^{\prime}f_{t}\leq\frac{CA}{1-t}.

In the end, we have

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}+\frac{C}{1+\alpha_{t}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}+\frac{C}{1-t}.

(83)

Control on
t\in[t_{0},1)
.
We have

\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}=\big|\partial_{t}\log\sigma_{t}\big|=\Big|-\frac{p}{1-t}+\frac{\ell^{\prime}(t)}{\ell(t)}\Big|\leq\frac{p}{1-t}+A^{2}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad t\in[t_{0},1).

Plugging this into (
83
)
yields

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad\text{for a.e. }t\in[t_{0},1).

Step 2:
t\in(0,t_{0}]
.

By assumption,
\sigma_{t}\geq A^{-1}
and
|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|\leq A
on
(0,t_{0}]
, hence

\frac{|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|}{\sigma_{t}}\leq C,\qquad t\in(0,t_{0}],

which combined with (
83
) gives the result.
∎

B.5.4
Proof of Corollary
6

Proof.

We apply Corollary
4
to the reduced model
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi
. In this setting one may take

\alpha_{t}=\frac{\alpha}{f_{t}^{2}},\qquad K_{t}=\frac{K}{f_{t}^{\beta}},\qquad L_{t}=\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}|}{f_{t}},

so that Corollary
4
yields, for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
with
\bar{g}_{t}>0
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|}{\bar{g}_{t}}+\frac{C}{1+\alpha_{t}\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\exp\Big(C_{0}K_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\bar{g}_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Big)\Big(\frac{|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|}{\bar{g}_{t}}+L_{t}\Big).

(84)

First, by Assumption
2
we have
g_{t}+f_{t}\geq\gamma
so

\frac{1}{1+\alpha_{t}\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}L_{t}=\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}|f_{t}}{f_{t}^{2}+\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\leq C\frac{A}{1-t}.

Next, the exponential factor in (
84
) is uniformly bounded on
(0,1)
:
since
\alpha_{t}+\bar{g}_{t}^{-2}\geq\alpha_{t}
, we have

K_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\bar{g}_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\leq K_{t}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{-\beta}=\frac{K^{2}}{f_{t}^{2\beta}}\Big(\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\alpha}\Big)^{\beta}=\frac{K^{2}}{\alpha^{\beta}},

hence

\exp\Big(C_{0}K_{t}^{2}(\alpha_{t}+\bar{g}_{t}^{-2})^{-\beta}\Big)\leq C.

In the end, we have

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|}{\bar{g}_{t}}+\frac{C}{1+\alpha_{t}\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\frac{|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|}{\bar{g}_{t}}+\frac{C}{1-t}.

(85)

Control on
[t_{0},1)
.

By assumption, there exist
t_{0}\in(0,1)
,
p>0
and a positive function
\ell
on
[t_{0},1)
such that

\bar{g}_{t}=(1-t)^{p}\ell(t),\qquad A^{-1}\leq\ell(t)\leq A,\qquad\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([t_{0},1))}\leq A.

Hence

\frac{|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|}{\bar{g}_{t}}=\big|\partial_{t}\log\bar{g}_{t}\big|=\left|-\frac{p}{1-t}+\frac{\ell^{\prime}(t)}{\ell(t)}\right|\leq\frac{p}{1-t}+A^{2},\qquad t\in[t_{0},1).

Moreover, since
f
is increasing with
f_{1}=1
, we have
f_{t}\geq f_{t_{0}}>0
on

[t_{0},1)
. Then
K_{t}
and the exponential factor in
(
85
) are bounded so we deduce that

\sup_{x}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad\text{for a.e. }t\in[t_{0},1).

(86)

Control on
(0,t_{0}]
.

Next, by Assumption
2
, there is
\delta>0
and
\gamma>0
such that

g_{t}\geq\gamma
for
t\in(0,\delta)
, and
\sigma
is increasing on
[0,\delta)
then decreasing on
(\delta,1)
with

\sigma_{0}=\sigma_{1}=0
. In particular, for
t\in[\delta,t_{0}]
one has
\sigma_{t}\geq\sigma_{t_{0}}>0
, and therefore

\bar{g}_{t}=\sqrt{g_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2}}\geq\min\{\gamma,\sigma_{t_{0}}\}\geq c^{-1},\qquad\forall t\in(0,t_{0}].

Plugging this into (
85
) yields for
t\in(0,t_{0}]
that

\sup_{x}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|+\frac{C}{1-t}.

Finally, since for all
t\in(0,t_{0})
,
|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|\leq A
we get the result.
∎

B.5.5
Proof of Corollary
7

Proof.

By the closed-form identity for the probability-flow drift in the diffusion setting,

v_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{t}\bigl(x+s(t,x)\bigr).

Moreover, the reduced model
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\sigma_{t}\xi
with
f_{t}=t
and
\sigma_{t}=\sqrt{1-t^{2}}
fits the
Lipman flow-matching framework, and the schedule assumptions of Corollary
5
are satisfied.
Therefore Corollary
5
yields

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t}.

∎

B.6
Details for time Lipschitz estimates

B.6.1
Proof of Theorem
4

Recall the reduced model of Theorem
4
:

X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi,

where
Y
has density
p(y)=e^{-u(y)+a(y)}
,
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, and
\xi
is independent of
Y
. For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we denote the posterior mean and covariance

\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x],\qquad\Sigma_{t}(x):=\text{Cov}(Y\mid X_{t}=x),

and we write
\rho^{t,x}
for the posterior law of
Y
given
X_{t}=x
, i.e.

\rho^{t,x}(dy)=\frac{p(y)\varphi^{x,\bar{g}_{t}}(f_{t}y)}{p_{t}(x)}dy,\qquad\varphi^{x,\sigma}(z):=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-d/2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\|x-z\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\Big).

(87)

A first key point is that, in the reduced model,
\dot{X}_{t}
is affine in
(Y,\xi)
, and therefore
v_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}[\dot{X}_{t}\mid X_{t}=x]
is an affine combination of
x
and the posterior mean
\mu_{t}(x)
. Differentiating this representation in time yields the following decomposition.

Proposition 13
.

Assume
f,\bar{g}\in W^{2,1}_{\text{loc}}(0,1)
and
\bar{g}_{t}>0
.
Then for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
at which
x\mapsto\mu_{t}(x)
is well-defined and
t\mapsto\mu_{t}(x)
is differentiable,

\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)=\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Big)x+\partial_{t}\Big(f_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t}\Big)\mu_{t}(x)+\Big(f_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t}\Big)\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x).

(88)

The proof of Proposition
13
can be found in Section
B.6.2
. We next control
\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)
.
The identity (
89
) is a conditional-differentiation (Fisher-type) formula: time variations of the posterior mean come from the time variations of the likelihood parameters
(f_{t},\bar{g}_{t})
.
The first term is the “Gaussian-linear” contribution: a covariance (hence of size
\gamma_{t}^{-1}
) multiplied by
x
.
The second term is a nonlinear correction involving the sensitivity of the posterior second moment
r_{t}(x)
.

Proposition 14
.

Let
p:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be an
(\alpha,\beta,K)
weakly log-concave probability density. Define

\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x],\qquad\Sigma_{t}(x):=\mathrm{Cov}(Y\mid X_{t}=x),\qquad r_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[\|Y\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x].

Set

a_{t}:=\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}},\qquad c_{t}:=f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t},\qquad\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}.

Then for a.e.
t\in I
and all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t}(x)x-c_{t}\nabla r_{t}(x).

(89)

Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of the dimension such that for all
t\in I
and all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\Sigma_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{C}{\gamma_{t}}\exp\Big(2K+\frac{CK^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}\Big)\leq C_{2}.

(90)

Consequently, for a.e.
t\in I
and all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|}{\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}}\|x\|+|c_{t}|\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|.

(91)

The proof of Proposition
14
can be found in Section
B.6.3
. The remaining task is therefore to control
\nabla r_{t}(x)
.
Since
r_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}[\|Y\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]
is a posterior expectation, differentiating in
x
naturally produces a covariance with the score of the Gaussian likelihood.
This turns
\nabla r_{t}(x)
into a third-order posterior quantity, expressed by the following identity.

Proposition 15
.

Fix
t
such that
f_{t}>0
and
\bar{g}_{t}>0
and assume
r_{t}(x)<\infty
.
Then, at every
x
where
r_{t}
is differentiable,

\nabla r_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\text{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\bigl(Y,\|Y\|^{2}\bigr).

The proof of Proposition
15
can be found in Section
B.6.4
. To bound this covariance, it is useful to compare the true posterior
\rho^{t,x}
(which is only weakly log-concave) to the log-concave surrogate posterior
\nu^{t,x}
obtained by removing the perturbation from the prior.
The surrogate
\nu^{t,x}
is genuinely strongly log-concave, with curvature
\gamma_{t}
, so it enjoys dimension-free concentration and Lipschitz dependence on
x
.
The next proposition shows that these properties are stable under the weak perturbation, and yields the desired affine bound on
\nabla r_{t}(x)
.

Proposition 16
.

Assume that
p^{\star}
is
(\alpha,\beta,K)
-weakly log-concave, let
\nu^{t,x}(dy)\propto\exp\left(-u(y)-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\right)dy

and set the curvature parameter
\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}.

Then there
C>0
independent of the dimension such that, at every
x
where
r_{t}
is differentiable,

\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|\leq A_{t}+B_{t}\|x\|,

with

B_{t}:=C\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{4}\gamma_{t}^{2}},\qquad A_{t}:=C\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\left(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}\right).

The proof of Proposition
16
can be found in Section
B.6.5
. Finally, we also need a direct affine bound on the posterior mean
\mu_{t}(x)
itself.
The slope in
\|x\|
again scales like
f_{t}/(\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t})
, which corresponds to the sensitivity of a
\gamma_{t}
-strongly log-concave posterior mean to changes in the observation.

Lemma 16
.

Let
Y
be a random variable on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with density

p(y)=e^{-u(y)+a(y)},
that is
(\alpha,\beta,K)
-weakly log concave
and let
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
be independent of
Y
. Set

\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[Y].

and the curvature parameter

\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}.

Then, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\Big(\|E_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\tfrac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|\Big).

The proof of Lemma
16
can be found in Section
B.6.6
.

Concluding the Proof of Theorem
4

Proof.

By Proposition
13
, for a.e.
t
we have

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq|a_{t}^{\prime}|\|x\|+|c_{t}^{\prime}|\|\mu_{t}(x)\|+|c_{t}|\|\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)\|.

(92)

Now, by Lemma
16

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\Big(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\tfrac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|\Big),

(93)

by Proposition
14

\|\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|}{\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}}\|x\|+|c_{t}|\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|.

(94)

and
By Proposition
16

\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|\leq C\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\Big(\|E_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\tfrac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}\Big)+C\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{4}\gamma_{t}^{2}}\|x\|.

(95)

Now, inserting (
93
) into (
92
), and using (
94
) we get

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq|a_{t}^{\prime}|\|x\|+|c_{t}^{\prime}|\|\mu_{t}(x)\|+|c_{t}|C\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|}{\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}}\|x\|+|c_{t}|^{2}\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|.

Now apply (
93
) and (
95
) to the last two terms involving

\mu_{t}(x)
and
\nabla r_{t}(x)
, and regroup the coefficients of
\|x\|
and the
\|x\|
-independent
terms. This yields precisely

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq\mathcal{A}_{t}+\mathcal{B}_{t}\|x\|,

with
\mathcal{A}_{t}
and
\mathcal{B}_{t}
as stated.
∎

B.6.2
Proof of Proposition
13

Proof.

Fix
t\in(0,1)
such that
f^{\prime}_{t},\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}
exist,
\bar{g}_{t}>0
, and such that

t\mapsto\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x]
is differentiable at
t
for the given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi
and
f,\bar{g}
depend only on
t
, we have

\dot{X}_{t}=f^{\prime}_{t}Y+\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}\xi,

therefore by linearity of conditional expectation,

v_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[\dot{X}_{t}\mid X_{t}=x]=f^{\prime}_{t}\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x]+\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}\mathbb{E}[\xi\mid X_{t}=x]=f^{\prime}_{t}\mu_{t}(x)+\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}\mathbb{E}[\xi\mid X_{t}=x].

Using the model identity
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi
, we can rewrite

\xi=\frac{X_{t}-f_{t}Y}{\bar{g}_{t}}.

Conditioning on
X_{t}=x
gives

\mathbb{E}[\xi\mid X_{t}=x]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{x-f_{t}Y}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Big|X_{t}=x\Big]=\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Big(x-f_{t}\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x]\Big)=\frac{x-f_{t}\mu_{t}(x)}{\bar{g}_{t}}.

Plugging this into the expression for
v_{t}
gives

v_{t}(x)=f^{\prime}_{t}\mu_{t}(x)+\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}\frac{x-f_{t}\mu_{t}(x)}{\bar{g}_{t}}=\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}x+\Big(f^{\prime}_{t}-\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t}\Big)\mu_{t}(x).

Define

a_{t}:=\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}},\qquad c_{t}:=f^{\prime}_{t}-a_{t}f_{t}=f^{\prime}_{t}-\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t},

so that
v_{t}(x)=a_{t}x+c_{t}\mu_{t}(x)
. Differentiating in
t
yields

\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)=a^{\prime}_{t}x+c^{\prime}_{t}\mu_{t}(x)+c_{t}\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x).

Finally, substituting back
a_{t}=\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}/\bar{g}_{t}
and

c_{t}=f^{\prime}_{t}-(\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}/\bar{g}_{t})f_{t}
gives

\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)=\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Big)x+\partial_{t}\Big(f^{\prime}_{t}-\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t}\Big)\mu_{t}(x)+\Big(f^{\prime}_{t}-\frac{\bar{g}^{\prime}_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}}f_{t}\Big)\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x),

which is exactly the claimed identity.
∎

B.6.3
Proof of Proposition
14

Proof.

For fixed
t
and
x
, the posterior
\rho^{t,x}
satisfies the covariance identity

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)=\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\Big(Y,\partial_{t}\log\varphi^{x,\bar{g}_{t}}(f_{t}Y)\Big).

(96)

A direct differentiation gives

\partial_{t}\log\varphi^{x,\bar{g}_{t}}(f_{t}Y)=\frac{f_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\langle x-f_{t}Y,Y\rangle+\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{3}}\Big(\|x-f_{t}Y\|^{2}-d\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\Big).

(97)

Since
\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\text{constant})=0
, the term
-d\bar{g}_{t}^{2}
in (
97
)
does not contribute. Expanding the remaining covariances yields

\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\big(Y,\langle x-f_{t}Y,Y\rangle\big)=\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\langle x,Y\rangle)-f_{t}\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2})=\Sigma_{t}(x)x-f_{t}\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2}),

and similarly

\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\big(Y,\|x-f_{t}Y\|^{2}\big)=-2f_{t}\Sigma_{t}(x)x+f_{t}^{2}\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2}),

because the
\|x\|^{2}
part is constant and vanishes under covariance.
Plugging these into (
96
)–(
97
) gives

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)=\Big(\frac{f_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}-\frac{2f_{t}\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{3}}\Big)\Sigma_{t}(x)x+\Big(-\frac{f_{t}f_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}+\frac{f_{t}^{2}\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{3}}\Big)\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2}).

It remains to identify
\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2})
with
\nabla r_{t}(x)
.
Write
r_{t}(x)=\int\|y\|^{2}\rho^{t,x}(dy)
. Differentiating under the integral sign and using the quotient rule,
one obtains the identity:

\nabla r_{t}(x)=\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\Big(\|Y\|^{2},\nabla_{x}\log\varphi^{x,\bar{g}_{t}}(f_{t}Y)\Big).

But
\nabla_{x}\log\varphi^{x,\bar{g}_{t}}(f_{t}Y)=\frac{f_{t}Y-x}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}
, hence the
-x
term drops in covariance and

\nabla r_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2}).

(98)

Therefore
\mathrm{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2})=\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}{f_{t}}\nabla r_{t}(x)
, and substituting this above yields

\partial_{t}\mu_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}^{\prime}-2(\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}/\bar{g}_{t})f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t}(x)x-\Big(f_{t}^{\prime}-(\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}/\bar{g}_{t})f_{t}\Big)\nabla r_{t}(x),

which is exactly (
89
). Now, fix
t\in I
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and a unit vector
h\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
Let
\nu^{t,x}
be the log-concave surrogate posterior

\nu^{t,x}(dy)\propto\exp\Big(-u(y)-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Big)dy,

so that
\rho^{t,x}(dy)\propto e^{a(y)}\nu^{t,x}(dy)
.
Define

L(y):=\frac{e^{a(y)}}{\int e^{a(z)}\nu^{t,x}(dz)}\quad\text{so that}\quad\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L]=1\text{ and }\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L\cdot].

Let
F(y):=h^{\top}y
. Then, using
\mathrm{Var}_{\rho}(F)=\inf_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(F-c)^{2}]
,

\mathrm{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(F)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\Big[L(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{2}\Big]\leq\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}.

Since
\nu^{t,x}
is log-concave on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the one-dimensional law of

F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F
is log-concave on
\mathbb{R}
, and there is a universal constant
C>0
such that

\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}\leq C\mathrm{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(F).

(99)

Moreover,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2a(Y)}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)}]^{2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y)}]^{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\big[e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}\big],

using Jensen to get
\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a-\mathbb{E}a}]\geq 1
.
Combining these bounds, we obtain

\mathrm{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y)\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}\Big)^{1/2}\mathrm{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y).

(100)

The negative log-density of
\nu^{t,x}
is

V_{t,x}(y)=u(y)+\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}},

so

\nabla^{2}V_{t,x}(y)=\nabla^{2}u(y)+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}I_{d}\succeq\Big(\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Big)I_{d}=\gamma_{t}\text{Id}\quad\text{uniformly in $y$ and $x$.}

The Brascamp–Lieb inequality applied to the linear function
y\mapsto h^{\top}y
gives

\mathrm{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y)\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}}.

(101)

Now, applying Lemma
8
we have

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\exp\Big(r(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))\Big)\leq\exp\Big(2Kr+\frac{CK^{2}}{2\gamma_{t}}r^{2}\Big)\qquad\forall r\geq 0,

so taking
r=2
and then square-roots yields

\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\exp\Big(2K+\frac{CK^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}\Big)\leq C.

(102)

Combining (
100
), (
101
), and (
102
) gives, for every unit
h
,

h^{\top}\Sigma_{t}(x)h=\mathrm{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y)\leq\frac{C}{\gamma_{t}}.

Taking the supremum over
h\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
yields (
90
).
Finally, insert (
90
) into (
89
):

\Big\|\frac{f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Sigma_{t}(x)x\Big\|\leq\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\|\Sigma_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|x\|\leq C\frac{|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|.

∎

B.6.4
Proof of Proposition
15

Proof.

Write the posterior density up to normalization as

\rho^{t,x}(dy)\propto p(y)\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)dy.

Let

Z(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}p(y)\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)dy,\qquad N(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|y\|^{2}p(y)\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)dy,

so that
r_{t}(x)=N(x)/Z(x)
. Then,

\nabla_{x}N(x)=\int\|y\|^{2}p(y)\nabla_{x}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigr)dy=-\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\int\|y\|^{2}(x-f_{t}y)p(y)e^{-\frac{\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}}dy.

Hence

\frac{\nabla_{x}N(x)}{Z(x)}=-\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}\bigl[(x-f_{t}Y)\|Y\|^{2}\bigr],\qquad\nabla_{x}\log Z(x)=\frac{\nabla_{x}Z(x)}{Z(x)}=-\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[x-f_{t}Y].

Therefore,

\displaystyle\nabla r_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla N(x)}{Z(x)}-r_{t}(x)\nabla\log Z(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[(x-f_{t}Y)\|Y\|^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[x-f_{t}Y]\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}\|Y\|^{2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigl(xr_{t}(x)-f_{t}\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[Y\|Y\|^{2}]-(x-f_{t}\mu_{t}(x))r_{t}(x)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[Y\|Y\|^{2}]-\mu_{t}(x)r_{t}(x)\Bigr)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\text{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}(Y,\|Y\|^{2}),

which gives the result.
∎

B.6.5
Proof of Proposition
16

Proof.

Fix
x
where
r_{t}
is differentiable and let
Y\sim\rho^{t,x}
.
By Proposition
15

\nabla r_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\text{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\big(Y,\|Y\|^{2}\big),

Hence for
h\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

h^{\top}\nabla r_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\text{Cov}_{\rho^{t,x}}\big(h^{\top}Y,\|Y\|^{2}\big).

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz we deduce that

\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\sqrt{\|\Sigma_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}}\sqrt{\text{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(\|Y\|^{2})}.

(103)

Write
\rho^{t,x}(dy)\propto e^{a(y)}\nu^{t,x}(dy)
and set

L(y):=\frac{e^{a(y)}}{\int e^{a}d\nu^{t,x}},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L]=1,\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L\cdot].

Let
F(y):=h^{\top}y
. Using
\text{Var}_{\rho}(F)=\inf_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(F-c)^{2}]
and choosing

c=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F
, one gets

\text{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(F)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\big[L(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{2}\big]\leq\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}.

Since
\nu^{t,x}
is log-concave, the one-dimensional law of

F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F
is log-concave on
\mathbb{R}
, hence

\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(F-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}F)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}\leq C\text{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(F)

(104)

for a universal constant
C>0
. Moreover

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2a(Y)}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)}]^{2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y)}]^{2}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))},

using Jensen in the denominator. Therefore,

\text{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y)\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a(Y)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(Y))}\Big)^{1/2}\text{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y).

Now
\nu^{t,x}
has negative log-density

V_{t,x}(y)=u(y)+\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}/(2\bar{g}_{t}^{2})
, hence

\nabla^{2}V_{t,x}\succeq\gamma_{t}\text{Id}
uniformly, so Brascamp–Lieb gives

\text{Var}_{\nu^{t,x}}(h^{\top}Y)\leq 1/\gamma_{t}
for
\|h\|=1
.
Combining and taking the supremum over
h
yields

\|\Sigma_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq C\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a)}\Big)^{1/2}\leq C\frac{M_{t,2}^{1/2}}{\gamma_{t}}.

(105)

With the same
L
as above and
G(y):=\|y\|^{2}
, we again use

\text{Var}_{\rho}(G)=\inf_{c}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}[(G-c)^{2}]
and choose
c=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}G
, giving

\text{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(G)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\big[L(G-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}G)^{2}\big]\leq\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}]\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(G-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}G)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}.

As above,
(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L^{2}])^{1/2}\leq(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}e^{2(a-\mathbb{E}a)})^{1/2}\leq M_{t,2}^{1/2}
,
so it remains to bound the fourth centered moment of
G
under
\nu^{t,x}
.

Let
U:=\|W\|
with
W\sim\nu^{t,x}
, set
m:=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}U
and
R:=U-m
.
Then
G-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}G=U^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}U^{2}=2mR+(R^{2}-\mathbb{E}R^{2})
.
Using
(a+b)^{4}\leq 8(a^{4}+b^{4})
, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(G-\mathbb{E}G)^{4}]\leq 8\cdot(2m)^{4}\mathbb{E}[R^{4}]+8\mathbb{E}[(R^{2}-\mathbb{E}R^{2})^{4}].

Since
\nu^{t,x}
is
\gamma_{t}
-strongly log-concave and
y\mapsto\|y\|
is
1
-Lipschitz,
Lemma
4
gives

\mathbb{E}[R^{4}]\leq C\gamma_{t}^{-2},\qquad\mathbb{E}[R^{8}]\leq C\gamma_{t}^{-4},\qquad\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]\leq C\gamma_{t}^{-1}.

Moreover,

\mathbb{E}[(R^{2}-\mathbb{E}R^{2})^{4}]\leq 8\mathbb{E}[R^{8}]+8(\mathbb{E}[R^{2}])^{4}\leq C\gamma_{t}^{-4}.

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(G-\mathbb{E}G)^{4}]\leq C\left(\frac{m^{4}}{\gamma_{t}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{4}}\right),\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(G-\mathbb{E}G)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}\leq C\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{2}}\right).

Finally,
m^{2}=(\mathbb{E}U)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}U^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}
by Jensen, so

\big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[(G-\mathbb{E}G)^{4}]\big)^{1/2}\leq C\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{2}}\right).

Plugging back yields

\text{Var}_{\rho^{t,x}}(\|W\|^{2})\leq CM_{t,2}^{1/2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{2}}\right).

(106)

Combining (
103
), (
105
), and (
106
) gives

\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\sqrt{C\frac{M_{t,2}^{1/2}}{\gamma_{t}}}\sqrt{CM_{t,2}^{1/2}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{2}}\right)}\leq CM_{t,2}^{1/2}\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}}{\gamma_{t}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{t}^{3/2}}\right).

It remains to bound
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}
by an affine function of
\|x\|
.
Let
\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[W]
and
\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}(x):=\text{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(W)
.
By Brascamp–Lieb,
\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}(x)\preceq\gamma_{t}^{-1}I_{d}
, hence

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}=\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|^{2}+\text{tr}(\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}(x))\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|^{2}+\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}.

Moreover, differentiating
\tilde{\mu}_{t}
with respect to
x
yields the Jacobian identity

\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}(x)
, hence

\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}
and therefore

\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(0)\|+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|.

Combining the last two displays and taking square-roots gives

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|W\|^{2}}\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(0)\|+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}.

Plugging this into the previous bound yields

\|\nabla r_{t}(x)\|\leq CM_{t,2}^{1/2}\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\left(\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(0)\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}\right)+CM_{t,2}^{1/2}\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{4}\gamma_{t}^{2}}\|x\|,

which is the claimed affine bound. Finally, applying Lemma
8
we have

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\exp\Big(2\big(a(W)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}a(W)\big)\Big)\leq\exp\left(4K+\frac{CK^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}\right).

∎

B.6.6
Proof of Lemma
16

Proof.

By Bayes’ formula, the posterior density of
Y
given
X_{t}=x
is

\rho^{t,x}(dy)\propto e^{-u(y)+a(y)}\exp\Big(-\tfrac{1}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}\Big)dy.

Since
\nu^{t,x}(dy)\propto e^{-u(y)}\exp(-\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}/(2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}))dy
, we can write

\rho^{t,x}(dy)=L_{t,x}(y)\nu^{t,x}(dy),\qquad L_{t,x}(y):=\frac{e^{a(y)}}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)}]}.

Therefore,

\mu_{t}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{t,x}}[Y]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L_{t,x}(Y)Y].

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz gives

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L_{t,x}(Y)^{2}]\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|Y\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

(107)

Let
\bar{a}_{x}:=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[a(Y)]
. Then

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L_{t,x}(Y)^{2}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2a(Y)}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{a(Y)}]^{2}}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2(a(Y)-\bar{a}_{x})}]}{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{(a(Y)-\bar{a}_{x})}]^{2}}.

By Jensen,
\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{(a(Y)-\bar{a}_{x})}]\geq e^{\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[a(Y)-\bar{a}_{x}]}=1
, hence applying Lemma
8

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L_{t,x}(Y)^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[e^{2(a(Y)-\bar{a}_{x})}]\leq\exp\left(4K+\frac{CK^{2}}{\gamma_{t}}\right).

Thus,

\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[L_{t,x}(Y)^{2}]\Big)^{1/2}\leq\exp\left(2K+\frac{CK^{2}}{2\gamma_{t}}\right)\leq C.

(108)

The density of
\nu^{t,x}
is proportional to
\exp(-V_{t,x}(y))
with

V_{t,x}(y)=u(y)+\frac{1}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}.

Since
\nabla^{2}u\succeq\alpha\text{Id}
and
\nabla^{2}_{y}\frac{1}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\|x-f_{t}y\|^{2}=\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}I_{d}
, we have

\nabla^{2}V_{t,x}(y)\succeq\Big(\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\Big)I_{d}=\gamma_{t}\text{Id},

so
\nu^{t,x}
is
\gamma_{t}
-strongly log-concave. By the Brascamp–Lieb inequality,

\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(Y)\preceq\gamma_{t}^{-1}I_{d}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathrm{tr}\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(Y)\leq\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}.

Hence, writing
\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}[Y]

\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|Y\|^{2}=\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|^{2}+\mathrm{tr}\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(Y)\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|^{2}+\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}.

(109)

Rewrite
\nu^{t,x}
as an exponential family in
x
:

\nu^{t,x}(dy)\propto\exp\Big(-u(y)-\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{2\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\|y\|^{2}+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}x\cdot y\Big)dy.

Differentiating under the integral sign (justified since
\nu^{t,x}
is strongly log-concave) yields the standard identity

\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)=\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(Y).

Therefore, using
\|\mathrm{Cov}_{\nu^{t,x}}(Y)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1/\gamma_{t}
,

\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}},

so
\tilde{\mu}_{t}
is Lipschitz and

\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(0)\|+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|.

(110)

Combining (
109
)–(
110
) and using
\sqrt{A^{2}+B}\leq|A|+\sqrt{B}
gives

\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\nu^{t,x}}\|Y\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(x)\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}\leq\|\tilde{\mu}_{t}(0)\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|.

Together with (
107
) and (
108
), this yields the claimed bound on
\|\mu_{t}(x)\|
.
∎

B.6.7
Proof of Corollary
8

Proof.

We apply Theorem
4
in the reduced model
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\sigma_{t}\xi
(so that
\bar{g}_{t}=\sigma_{t}
). Define

a_{t}:=\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}},\qquad c_{t}:=f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t},\qquad\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}},\qquad D_{t}:=\alpha\sigma_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}=\sigma_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}.

Then Theorem
4
yields, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
,

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq A_{t}+B_{t}\|x\|.

Control on
[t_{0},1)
.

Since
f
is increasing we have
f_{t}\geq f_{t_{0}}
for all
t\in[t_{0},1)
. Then on
[t_{0},1)
,

D_{t}\geq f_{t}^{2}\geq C^{-1},\qquad\frac{1}{D_{t}}\leq C,\qquad\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\leq C,\qquad\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{D_{t}^{2}}\leq C,

so every ratio involving
D_{t}
which appears in
A_{t},B_{t}
is uniformly bounded on
[t_{0},1)
.
Using
A^{-1}\leq\ell\leq A
and
\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
, we get
|a_{t}|\leq C(1-t)^{-1}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Differentiating again,

a_{t}^{\prime}=-\frac{p}{(1-t)^{2}}+\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\ell^{\prime}}{\ell}\Bigr),

and
\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\|\ell^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
,
A^{-1}\leq\ell\leq A
give
|a_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim(1-t)^{-2}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Using
0\leq f_{t}\leq 1
and
\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
on
[t_{0},1)
,

|c_{t}|=|f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}||f_{t}|\lesssim 1+\frac{1}{1-t}\lesssim\frac{1}{1-t}.

Moreover,

c_{t}^{\prime}=f_{t}^{\prime\prime}-a_{t}^{\prime}f_{t}-a_{t}f_{t}^{\prime},

so
\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
and the bounds on
a_{t},a_{t}^{\prime}
yield
|c_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim(1-t)^{-2}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Finally,

|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+2|a_{t}||f_{t}|\lesssim 1+\frac{1}{1-t}\lesssim\frac{1}{1-t}.

Inspecting the expressions of Theorem
4
,
A_{t}
is a sum of terms of the form

|c_{t}^{\prime}|\times(\text{bounded ratios in }D_{t})\cdot\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr)

and

|c_{t}|^{2}\times(\text{bounded ratios in }D_{t})\cdot\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr),

while
B_{t}
is a sum of terms of the form

|a_{t}^{\prime}|+|c_{t}^{\prime}|\times(\text{bounded ratios in }D_{t})+|c_{t}|\cdot|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|\times(\text{bounded ratios in }D_{t})+|c_{t}|^{2}\times(\text{bounded ratios in }D_{t}).

All ratios in
D_{t}
are uniformly bounded on
[t_{0},1)
whereas

|a_{t}^{\prime}|,|c_{t}^{\prime}|,|c_{t}|^{2}
, and
|c_{t}||f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|
are all
O((1-t)^{-2})
. Therefore for a.e.
t\in[t_{0},1)
,

A_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr),\qquad B_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}.

Using
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
and Lemma
10
, we have

\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|\leq\|\argmin u\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha+\lambda_{t}}}\leq A\sqrt{d}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\sqrt{d},

so

A_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\Bigl(A\sqrt{d}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha}}\Bigr)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{(1-t)^{2}}\sqrt{d}.

Control on
(0,t_{0}]
.

By assumption, for all
t\in(0,t_{0}]
we have
\sigma_{t}^{-1}\leq A
and
|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|+|\sigma_{t}^{\prime\prime}|\leq A
, so

|a_{t}|=\Bigl|\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\Bigr|\leq|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|\sigma_{t}^{-1}\leq A^{2}.

Moreover, since
\sigma\in W^{2,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(0,1)
and
\sigma_{t}>0
, we have for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
,

a_{t}^{\prime}=\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\Bigr)=\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}-\Bigl(\frac{\sigma_{t}^{\prime}}{\sigma_{t}}\Bigr)^{2},

hence
|a_{t}^{\prime}|\leq|\sigma_{t}^{\prime\prime}|\sigma_{t}^{-1}+|\sigma_{t}^{\prime}|^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}\leq A^{2}+A^{4}
.
Similarly,

|c_{t}|=|f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}|\leq A+A^{2},\qquad|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+2|a_{t}|\leq A+2A^{2},

and using
c_{t}^{\prime}=f_{t}^{\prime\prime}-a_{t}^{\prime}f_{t}-a_{t}f_{t}^{\prime}
with
|f_{t}|\leq 1
,

|c_{t}^{\prime}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime\prime}|+|a_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}||f_{t}^{\prime}|\leq A+(A^{2}+A^{4})+A^{3}.

Therefore, for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
,
B_{t}
is bounded by a constant depending only on
\alpha,\beta,K
and
A
,
whereas
A_{t}
satisfies

A_{t}\leq C_{0}\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr)

for some
C_{0}>0
with the same dependencies. Using
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
and Lemma
10
,

\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|\leq\|\argmin u\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha+\lambda_{t}}}\leq A\sqrt{d}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha}}\sqrt{d},

we obtain
A_{t}\leq C_{0}^{\prime}\sqrt{d}
for some
C_{0}^{\prime}>0
.
Hence, for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
and all
x
,

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq A_{t}+B_{t}\|x\|\leq C_{0}^{\prime}\bigl(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\bigr).

Combining with the estimate on
[t_{0},1)
gives the claim.
∎

B.6.8
Proof of Corollary
9

Proof.

We apply Theorem
4
to the reduced model
X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi
. Define, for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
,

a_{t}:=\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}},\qquad c_{t}:=f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t},\qquad\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}},\qquad D_{t}:=\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}=\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}.

Then Theorem
4
yields: for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
,

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq A_{t}+B_{t}\|x\|,

where
A_{t},B_{t}
are the explicit quantities in Theorem
4
.

Control on
[t_{0},1)
.

Since
f
is increasing we have
f_{t}\geq f_{t_{0}}

for all
t\in[t_{0},1)
. Then on
[t_{0},1)
,

D_{t}\geq f_{t}^{2}\geq C^{-1},\qquad\frac{1}{D_{t}}\leq C,\qquad\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\leq C,\qquad\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{D_{t}^{2}}\leq C,

so every ratio involving
D_{t}
which appears in
A_{t},B_{t}
is uniformly bounded on
[t_{0},1)
. Next, from
\bar{g}_{t}=(1-t)^{p}\ell(t)
we have

a_{t}=\partial_{t}(\log\bar{g}_{t})=-\frac{p}{1-t}+\frac{\ell^{\prime}(t)}{\ell(t)}.

Using
A^{-1}\leq\ell\leq A
and
\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
, we get
|a_{t}|\leq C(1-t)^{-1}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Differentiating again,

a_{t}^{\prime}=\frac{p}{(1-t)^{2}}+\partial_{t}\Bigl(\frac{\ell^{\prime}}{\ell}\Bigr),

and
\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\|\ell^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
,
A^{-1}\leq\ell\leq A
give
|a_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim(1-t)^{-2}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Using
0\leq f_{t}\leq 1
and
\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
on
[t_{0},1)
,

|c_{t}|=|f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}||f_{t}|\lesssim 1+\frac{1}{1-t}\lesssim\frac{1}{1-t}.

Moreover,

c_{t}^{\prime}=f_{t}^{\prime\prime}-a_{t}^{\prime}f_{t}-a_{t}f_{t}^{\prime},

so
\|f^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A
and the bounds on
a_{t},a_{t}^{\prime}
yield
|c_{t}^{\prime}|\lesssim(1-t)^{-2}
on
[t_{0},1)
.
Finally,

|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+2|a_{t}||f_{t}|\lesssim\frac{1}{1-t}.

Inspecting the expressions of
A_{t}
and
B_{t}
in Theorem
4
, and using that on
[t_{0},1)

all ratios in
D_{t}
, we conclude that
A_{t}
is a sum of terms of size

\lesssim\Bigl(|c_{t}^{\prime}|+|c_{t}|^{2}\Bigr)\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr)+|c_{t}|^{2}\Bigl(\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}-\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr),

and in particular

A_{t}\lesssim\Bigl(|c_{t}^{\prime}|+|c_{t}|^{2}\Bigr)\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr).

Moreover
B_{t}
is a sum of terms of size

\lesssim|a_{t}^{\prime}|+|c_{t}^{\prime}|+|c_{t}||f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|+|c_{t}|^{2}
.
Hence, on
[t_{0},1)
,

A_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr),\qquad B_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}.

Using
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
and Lemma
10
with
y_{0}:=\arg\min u
, we have

\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|\leq\|y_{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha+\lambda_{t}}}\leq\|y_{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha}}\leq(A+\alpha^{-1/2})\sqrt{d},

where we used the hypothesis
\|y_{0}\|\leq A\sqrt{d}
.
Hence
A_{t}\leq\dfrac{C^{\prime}}{(1-t)^{2}}\sqrt{d}
for some
C^{\prime}>0
.

Control on
(0,t_{0}]
.

By Assumption
2
,
f_{t},g_{t},\sigma_{t}\in[0,1]
, hence
\bar{g}_{t}\leq\sqrt{2}
for all
t\in(0,1)
.
On
(0,t_{0}]
, the additional assumption (4) gives
\bar{g}_{t}^{-1}\leq A
, so
\bar{g}_{t}
is bounded above and below
by constants depending only on
A
. In particular,

\gamma_{t}=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}\geq\alpha,

and all rational expressions in
\bar{g}_{t},f_{t},\gamma_{t},D_{t}
appearing in
A_{t},B_{t}
are bounded
by constants depending only on
\alpha
and
A
.

Next, for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
, by (4),

|a_{t}|=\Bigl|\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Bigr|\leq|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|\bar{g}_{t}^{-1}\leq A^{2},\qquad|a_{t}^{\prime}|=\Bigl|\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}-\Bigl(\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr|\leq|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime\prime}|\bar{g}_{t}^{-1}+|a_{t}|^{2}\leq A^{2}+A^{4}.

Similarly,

|c_{t}|=|f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}|\leq A+A^{2},

|f_{t}^{\prime}-2a_{t}f_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+2|a_{t}|\leq A+2A^{2},

and using
c_{t}^{\prime}=f_{t}^{\prime\prime}-a_{t}^{\prime}f_{t}-a_{t}f_{t}^{\prime}
with
|f_{t}|\leq 1
,

|c_{t}^{\prime}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime\prime}|+|a_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}||f_{t}^{\prime}|\leq A+(A^{2}+A^{4})+A^{3}.

Therefore, for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
,
B_{t}
is bounded by a constant depending only on

\alpha,\beta,K
and
A
, whereas
A_{t}
satisfies

A_{t}\leq C_{0}\Bigl(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\Bigr)

for some
C_{0}>0
with the same dependencies.
Using
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
and Lemma
10
with
y_{0}:=\arg\min u
, we have

\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|\leq\|y_{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha+\lambda_{t}}}\leq\|y_{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha}}\leq(A+\alpha^{-1/2})\sqrt{d},

where we used the hypothesis
\|y_{0}\|\leq A\sqrt{d}
.
Therefore
A_{t}\leq C_{0}^{\prime}\sqrt{d}
for some
C_{0}^{\prime}>0
.
Hence, for a.e.
t\in(0,t_{0}]
and all
x
,

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq A_{t}+B_{t}\|x\|\leq C_{0}^{\prime}(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|).

∎

B.6.9
Proof of Corollary
10

Proof.

Let
v_{t}
denote the (rescaled) probability flow drift in the diffusion setting. By the closed-form identity

v_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{t}\big(x+s(t,x)\big),

we have

\partial_{t}s(t,x)=v_{t}(x)+t\partial_{t}v_{t}(x).

The reduced model
X_{t}=tY+\sigma_{t}\xi
fits the Lipman flow matching setting of Assumption
1
.
Moreover, the schedule assumptions of Corollary
8
are satisfied: take
t_{0}=1/2
and write

\sigma_{t}=(1-t)^{1/2}\ell(t)
with
\ell(t)=\sqrt{1+t}
, so that
\ell,\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime}
are bounded on
[t_{0},1)
,
and note that
f_{t}=t
satisfies
f^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime}\in L^{\infty}
, while
\sigma^{-1},|\sigma^{\prime}|,|\sigma^{\prime\prime}|
are bounded on
(0,t_{0}]
.
Therefore Corollary
8
applies and yields that for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
and all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big).

Let
\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x]
. Using the identity

\mu_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{t}\Big(x+(1-t^{2})s(t,x)\Big),

we obtain

v_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{t}(x+s(t,x))=\frac{\mu_{t}(x)-tx}{1-t^{2}}.

We now bound
\mu_{t}(x)
using Lemma
16
(applied with
f_{t}=t
and
\bar{g}_{t}=\sigma_{t}
). Writing

\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}=\alpha+\frac{t^{2}}{1-t^{2}},

Lemma
16
gives, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\Big(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[Y]\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{t}{\sigma_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|\Big).

Since
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
and
\sigma_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}=\alpha(1-t^{2})+t^{2}=\alpha+(1-\alpha)t^{2}\geq\alpha\wedge 1
,
the exponential factor is uniformly bounded and
\frac{t}{\sigma_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\leq(\alpha\wedge 1)^{-1}
.
Using the assumption
\|\argmin u\|d^{-1/2}\leq A
we obtain by lemma
10

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big).

Consequently,

\|v_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{\|\mu_{t}(x)\|+t\|x\|}{1-t^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t^{2}}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big)\leq\frac{C}{1-t}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big).

Combining
\partial_{t}s(t,x)=v_{t}(x)+t\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)
with the two bounds above yields, for a.e.
t\in(0,1)
,

\|\partial_{t}s(t,x)\|\leq\frac{C}{1-t}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big)+\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big)\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\big(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\big),

which proves the claim.
∎

B.7
Technical novelties for the global and time Lipschitz estimates

As the literature on regularity of exact drifts is already large, we only discuss the closest works, namely
4
,
16
,
17
, and
20
. These papers all obtain regularity information on flow-matching vector fields or diffusion-model scores, but they do so through rather different mechanisms.

4
start from an explicit Jacobian formula for the flow-matching vector field and estimate it through assumptions formulated directly on posterior covariances.
16
also use an explicit representation of the vector field, but their argument controls the derivatives through a hierarchy of conditional moments together with model-specific regularity assumptions. In the diffusion setting,
17
exploit Ornstein-Uhlenbeck regularization and Hessian identities for the smoothed density.
20
, on the other hand, studies a covariance representation of the vector field and derives from it an integrated control of the Lipschitz constant under explicit covariance asymptotics.

Although several recent works obtain regularity estimates for flow-matching vector fields or diffusion-model scores, the available proof strategies are quite heterogeneous and remain closely tied to the regimes for which they were designed. For this reason, they do not provide a robust route to the present general setting, where the target is only weakly log-concave and the perturbation is merely Hölder. In that regime, one cannot expect a direct differentiation-based argument, nor a closure through model-specific moment expansions, to yield sharp pointwise-in-time bounds with the optimal dimension dependence. In contrast, our approach recasts both the global space-Lipschitz and time-Lipschitz estimates as stability problems for tilted strongly log-concave posterior laws. This avoids both model-specific moment closures and direct PDE/Hessian analyses of the smoothed density, and is precisely what allows us to work under weak log-concavity with merely Hölder perturbations.

The first key ingredient is Lemma
7
. For each time
t\in(0,1)
and position
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we introduce the strongly log-concave surrogate posterior law

\nu_{t,x}(dy)\propto e^{-a(y)}p^{t,x}(y)dy,

with
p^{t,x}=\mathrm{Law}(m_{t}(Z)\mid X_{t}=x)
.
Lemma
7
studies the log-partition function

a_{t}(x):=\log\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,x}}\big[e^{a(Y)}\big],

where
a
is the Hölder perturbation in the decomposition
p^{\star}=e^{-u+a}
. Its key new point is an
L^{p}
log-Laplace stability estimate, which shows that the map
x\mapsto a_{t}(x)
remains Hölder without ever differentiating the rough term
a
. The second key ingredient is Proposition
16
, which gives an affine bound on
\nabla r_{t}(x)
, where

r_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[\|Y\|^{2}\mid X_{t}=x]

is the posterior second moment of the endpoint variable
Y
given the intermediate state
X_{t}=x
. In particular, the time-Lipschitz bound is not obtained by estimating separately a large hierarchy of conditional moments.

Starting from the posterior representations, the whole argument is reduced to a small family of stable posterior quantities. Proposition
9
isolates the explicit Gaussian singularity and leaves only covariance terms, which are compared to their strongly log-concave surrogates through Lemma
7
and Lemma
8
. Then, Proposition
13
rewrites
\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)
in terms of the posterior mean, Proposition
14
reduces the problem to
\nabla r_{t}(x)
, and Propositions
15
-
16
together with Lemma
16
close the estimate by the same surrogate-posterior comparison mechanism.

Overall, the novelty is twofold. First, the paper introduces a reusable proof architecture: posterior representation, removal of the rough tilt, analysis under a strongly log-concave surrogate, and transfer back to the true posterior through quantitative stability estimates. Second, this architecture is made effective by two genuinely new estimates: the covariance comparison built from Lemma
7
and Lemma
8
, and the affine control of
\nabla r_{t}
in Proposition
16
. Together, these ingredients are what allow the paper to obtain sharp pointwise-in-time bounds, with dimension-free leading constants, in a regime of weak log-concavity and Hölder perturbations that lies beyond the reach of the proof strategies discussed above.

B.8
Proofs of the functional inequalities

B.8.1
Proof of Lemma
2

Proof.

Differentiating the flow equation with respect to
x
gives

\partial_{t}\nabla X_{t}(x)=\nabla v_{t}(X_{t}(x))\nabla X_{t}(x),\qquad\nabla X_{0}(x)=\text{Id}.

Fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a unit vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and set

w_{t}:=\nabla X_{t}(x)h,\qquad a_{t}:=|w_{t}|^{2}.

Then

\partial_{t}a_{t}=2\langle w_{t},\partial_{t}w_{t}\rangle=2\langle w_{t},\nabla v_{t}(X_{t}(x))w_{t}\rangle.

By the definition of
\lambda_{\max}
,

\langle w_{t},\nabla v_{t}(X_{t}(x))w_{t}\rangle\leq\lambda_{\max}\big(\nabla v_{t}(X_{t}(x))\big)|w_{t}|^{2}\leq\theta_{t}a_{t}.

Hence

\partial_{t}a_{t}\leq 2\theta_{t}a_{t}.

By Grönwall’s inequality,

a_{t}\leq a_{0}\exp\Big(2\int_{0}^{t}\theta_{s}ds\Big)=\exp\Big(2\int_{0}^{t}\theta_{s}ds\Big),

since
a_{0}=|\nabla X_{0}(x)h|^{2}=|h|^{2}=1
.
This yields uniformly in
x
,

\|\nabla X_{t}(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sup_{|h|=1}|\nabla X_{t}(x)h|\leq\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\theta_{s}ds\Big).

∎

B.8.2
Proof of Proposition
5

Proof.

Define
F:=\zeta\circ T
. Since
T_{\#}\gamma_{d}=p
, we have

\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p}(\zeta)=\text{Ent}_{\Psi,\gamma_{d}}(F).

Applying (
20
) to
F
gives

\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p}(\zeta)=\text{Ent}_{\Psi,\gamma_{d}}(F)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{\prime\prime}(F(y))|\nabla F(y)|^{2}d\gamma_{d}(y).

As
|\nabla F(y)|\leq L|\nabla\zeta(T(y))|
, hence

\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p}(\zeta)\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta(T(y)))|\nabla\zeta(T(y))|^{2}d\gamma_{d}(y).

Using again
T_{\#}\gamma_{d}=p
and the change of variables
x=T(y)
yields

\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p}(\zeta)\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{\prime\prime}(\zeta(x))|\nabla\zeta(x)|^{2}p(x)dx,

as claimed.
∎

B.8.3
Proof of Theorem
6

Proof.

Let

(X_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
be the flow associated with the flow-matching vector field
(v_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
from the probability flow ODE, namely

\partial_{t}X_{t}(x)=v_{t}\bigl(X_{t}(x)\bigr),\qquad X_{0}(x)=x,

and define the terminal map
T:=X_{1}
. The probability flow ODE considers the model

X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\sigma_{t}\xi
with
(Y,\xi)\sim p^{\star}\otimes\gamma_{d}
,
f_{t}=t
and
\sigma_{t}=\sqrt{1-t^{2}}
.
As shown in the proof of Corollary
3
,
this choice satisfies the hypotheses required to apply Corollary
13
to the associated
flow-matching vector field
v_{t}
.

By Corollary
13
, the map
T
pushes forward the standard Gaussian measure
\gamma_{d}
onto
p^{\star}

and is globally Lipschitz with a dimension-free bound

\text{Lip}(T)\leq e^{C_{\mathrm{flow}}}=:L.

Applying Proposition
5
to the pushforward relation
T_{\#}\gamma_{d}=p^{\star}
and the Lipschitz bound
\text{Lip}(T)\leq L
,
we obtain

\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p^{\star}}(f)\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Psi^{\prime\prime}(f(x))\|\nabla f(x)\|^{2}p^{\star}(x)dx.

•

Poincaré.
Take
\Psi(u)=u^{2}
on
\mathbb{R}
. Then
\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p^{\star}}(f)=\text{Var}_{p^{\star}}(f)
and
\Psi^{\prime\prime}\equiv 2
,
hence for smooth
f
,

\text{Var}_{p^{\star}}(f)\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int 2\|\nabla f\|^{2}dp^{\star}=L^{2}\int\|\nabla f\|^{2}dp^{\star}.

•

Log-Sobolev.
Take
\Psi(u)=u\log u
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
and set
\zeta=f^{2}
with
f
smooth.
Then
\text{Ent}_{\Psi,p^{\star}}(f^{2})=\text{Ent}_{p^{\star}}(f^{2})
and
\Psi^{\prime\prime}(u)=1/u
. Therefore,

\text{Ent}_{p^{\star}}(f^{2})\leq\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int\frac{1}{f^{2}}\|\nabla(f^{2})\|^{2}dp^{\star}=\frac{L^{2}}{2}\int\frac{1}{f^{2}}4f^{2}\|\nabla f\|^{2}dp^{\star}=2L^{2}\int\|\nabla f\|^{2}dp^{\star}.

∎

Appendix C
Proofs of the applications to sampling

C.1
Proof of the discretization errors

C.1.1
Proof of Proposition
1

Proof.

By synchronous coupling (same
X_{0}
and same Brownian motion
W
),

W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N}))\leq\Big(\mathbb{E}\|X_{\tau}-\bar{X}_{N}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

Set

\Delta_{k}:=X_{t_{k}}-\bar{X}_{k}.

Step 1: restart the exact flow at each grid point.

For each
k
, let
Y^{(k)}
be the solution on
[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
of

dY^{(k)}_{t}=a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})dt+b_{t}dW_{t},\qquad Y^{(k)}_{t_{k}}=\bar{X}_{k}.

Then

\|\Delta_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|X_{t_{k+1}}-Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}-\bar{X}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

(111)

Step 2: stability with respect to the initial condition.

Let
Z_{t}:=X_{t}-Y^{(k)}_{t}
on
[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
. Since both SDEs have the same diffusion term,

dZ_{t}=(a_{t}(X_{t})-a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t}))dt,

so

\frac{d}{dt}\|Z_{t}\|^{2}=2\langle Z_{t},a_{t}(X_{t})-a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})\rangle\leq 2L_{t}\|Z_{t}\|^{2}

by (A1). Applying Grönwall’s lemma, we get

\|Z_{t_{k+1}}\|^{2}\leq\exp\Big(2\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds\Big)\|Z_{t_{k}}\|^{2}=\exp\Big(2\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds\Big)\|\Delta_{k}\|^{2}.

Taking expectations and square roots,

\|X_{t_{k+1}}-Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\exp\Big(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds\Big)\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

(112)

Step 3: stability with respect to the drift.

From the definitions of
Y^{(k)}
and the Euler update (
15
),

\displaystyle Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}-\bar{X}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big)dt+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}b_{t}dW_{t}-\xi_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big)dt,

so by Minkowski,

\displaystyle\|Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}-\bar{X}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\|a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\|_{L^{2}}dt.

(113)

Split

a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})=\big(a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})\big)+\big(a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big).

(114)

Set

A_{k}:=\sup_{t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}]}\|Y^{(k)}_{t}\|_{L^{2}},

from

Y^{(k)}_{t}=\bar{X}_{k}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})ds+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}b_{s}dW_{s}

and Minkowski plus Itô isometry,

A_{k}\leq\|\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\|a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})\|_{L^{2}}ds+B\sqrt{d}\sqrt{h_{k}}.

By (A4),
\|a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})\|_{L^{2}}\leq F_{s}(\sqrt{d}+\|Y^{(k)}_{s}\|_{L^{2}})\leq F_{s}(\sqrt{d}+A_{k})
, hence

A_{k}\leq\|\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+(\sqrt{d}+A_{k})\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{s}ds+B\sqrt{d}\sqrt{h_{k}}.

As by assumption
\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{s}ds\leq 1/2
and
\|\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq A_{\bar{X}}
, we obtain

A_{k}\leq 2(A_{\bar{X}}+\sqrt{d}+B\sqrt{dh_{k}})=:U_{k}-2\sqrt{d}.

Time variation term.

Fix
t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
, we have,

a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})=\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\partial_{s}a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{t})ds,

hence by (A3)

\|a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\Big(\int_{t_{k}}^{t}M_{s}ds\Big)\Big(\sqrt{d}+\|Y^{(k)}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\leq\Big(\int_{t_{k}}^{t}M_{s}ds\Big)U_{k}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\|a_{t}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq h_{k}U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}M_{t}dt.

Space variation term.

By (A2),

\|a_{t_{k}}(Y^{(k)}_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{t_{k}}\|Y^{(k)}_{t}-\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

Moreover, for
t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
,

Y^{(k)}_{t}-\bar{X}_{k}=\int_{t_{k}}^{t}a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})ds+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}b_{s}dW_{s},

so by Minkowski and Itô isometry,

\|Y^{(k)}_{t}-\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\|a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})\|_{L^{2}}ds+B\sqrt{d}\sqrt{t-t_{k}}.

Using (A4) and
\|Y^{(k)}_{s}\|_{L^{2}}\leq U_{k}-\sqrt{d}
, we have

\|a_{s}(Y^{(k)}_{s})\|_{L^{2}}\leq F_{s}U_{k}
, hence

\|Y^{(k)}_{t}-\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}F_{s}ds+B\sqrt{d}\sqrt{t-t_{k}},

which gives

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\|Y^{(k)}_{t}-\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L^{2}}dt\leq h_{k}U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{t}dt+\frac{2}{3}B\sqrt{d}h_{k}^{3/2}.

Plugging into (
113
) yields

\|Y^{(k)}_{t_{k+1}}-\bar{X}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\rho_{k},

(115)

where

\rho_{k}:=h_{k}U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}M_{t}dt+h_{k}C_{t_{k}}\Big(U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{t}dt+\frac{2}{3}B\sqrt{d}h_{k}^{1/2}\Big).

Step 4: discrete Grönwall.

Combining (
111
), (
112
), and (
115
),

\|\Delta_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\exp\Big(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds\Big)\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\rho_{k}.

Iterating,

\|\Delta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\rho_{k}\prod_{j=k+1}^{N-1}\exp\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}L_{s}ds\Big)=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\rho_{k}\exp\Big(\int_{t_{k+1}}^{\tau}L_{s}ds\Big).

Finally,
W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N}))\leq\|\Delta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}
, giving the result.
∎

C.1.2
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof.

Proposition
1
in the ODE case yields

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\exp\Big(\int_{t_{k+1}}^{\tau}L_{s}ds\Big)\Bigg[U_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(M_{t}+C_{t_{k}}F_{t}\big)dt\Bigg],

(116)

with
U_{k}=2A_{\bar{X}}+4\sqrt{d}\leq C\sqrt{d}
(since
B=0
). Then, (
116
) gives

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq C\sqrt{d}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(M_{t}+C_{t_{k}}F_{t}\big)dt.

(117)

Now, as
t_{k}=1-r^{k}
,

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{t}dt\leq A\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{dt}{1-t}=A\log\Big(\frac{1-t_{k}}{1-t_{k+1}}\Big)=A\log\Big(\frac{r^{k}}{r^{k+1}}\Big)=A\log\Big(\frac{1}{1-h_{\max}}\Big).

(118)

Thus choosing
\bar{h}\in(0,1)
such that

A\log\big(\frac{1}{1-\bar{h}}\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}

ensures
\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{t}dt\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
k
whenever
h_{\max}\leq\bar{h}
.
Using (
16
),

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}M_{t}dt\leq A\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{dt}{(1-t)^{2}}=A\Big(\frac{1}{1-t_{k+1}}-\frac{1}{1-t_{k}}\Big)=A\Big(\frac{1}{r^{k+1}}-\frac{1}{r^{k}}\Big)=A\frac{1-r}{r^{k+1}}.

Multiplying by
h_{k}=r^{k}(1-r)=r^{k}h_{\max}
yields

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}M_{t}dt\leq NA\frac{h_{\max}^{2}}{1-h_{\max}}.

Since
r^{N}=1-\tau
and
r=1-h_{\max}
, we have

N=\frac{\log(1/(1-\tau))}{\log(1/r)}=\frac{\log(1/(1-\tau))}{\log(1/(1-h_{\max}))}\leq\frac{\log(1/(1-\tau))}{h_{\max}},

using
-\log(1-h_{\max})\geq h_{\max}
. Hence for
h_{\max}\leq 1/2
,

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}M_{t}dt\leq Ch_{\max}\log\Big(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Big).

(119)

Using again (
16
) and (
118
),

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}F_{t}dt\leq A\log(1/r).

Also
C_{t_{k}}\leq\frac{A}{1-t_{k}}
so

h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}C_{t_{k}}F_{t}dt\leq h_{k}\frac{A^{2}}{r^{k}}\log(1/r),

which gives

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}C_{t_{k}}F_{t}dt

\displaystyle\leq A^{2}Nh_{\max}\log(1/r)

\displaystyle\leq Ch_{\max}\log(1/(1-\tau)).

Combining (
117
) and (
119
), we obtain for

h_{\max}\leq\min(\bar{h},1/2)

\displaystyle W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\log\Big(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Big).

∎

C.1.3
Proof of Theorem
5

Let us first state an intermediate decomposition.
Define for
k\in\{0,...,N-1\}

\Delta_{k}:=X_{t_{k}}-\bar{X}_{k},\qquad V_{k}:=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\Big(a_{t}(X_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})\Big)dt.

(120)

Proposition 17
.

Suppose we are under the assumptions of Theorem
5
and
assume that there exists
L\in L^{1}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R})
such that for all
k
,

\big\|\Delta_{k}+h_{k}\big(a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds}\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}.

(121)

Writing
\mathcal{F}_{k}:=\sigma(X_{t_{k}},\bar{X}_{k})
, assume
there exist
\mathrm{Bias}_{k},\mathrm{Var}_{k}\geq 0
such that

\big\|\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq\mathrm{Bias}_{k},\qquad\big\|V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq\mathrm{Var}_{k}.

(122)

Then, the discretization error satisfies

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\exp\Big(\int_{t_{k+1}}^{\tau}L_{u}du\Big)\mathrm{Bias}_{k}+\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\exp\Big(2\int_{t_{k+1}}^{\tau}L_{u}du\Big)\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

The proof of Proposition
17
can be found in Section
C.1.5
. We can now give the proof of Theorem
5
.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

We work on the same probability space and use the synchronous coupling driven by the same Brownian motion.
Hence

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq\|X_{\tau}-\bar{X}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}.

(123)

Let
\Delta_{k}
and
V_{k}
defined in (
C.1.3
),
using
\xi_{k+1}=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}b_{t}dW_{t}
gives

\Delta_{k+1}=\Delta_{k}+h_{k}\Bigl(a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\Bigr)+V_{k}.

(124)

Step 1: stability of the one-step map.

We have
\lambda_{\max}(\nabla a_{t}(\cdot))\leq L_{t}
and
\lambda_{\min}(\nabla a_{t_{k}})\geq-C_{t_{k}}
.
By (
17
),
C_{t_{k}}\leq A/\rho(t_{k})
, hence

h_{k}C_{t_{k}}\leq A\frac{h_{k}}{\rho(t_{k})}.

We bound
\frac{h_{k}}{\rho(t_{k})}
uniformly over
k
.
If
T-t_{k}\geq 1
, then
\rho(t_{k})=1
and
h_{k}/\rho(t_{k})=h_{k}\leq h_{\max}
.
If
T-t_{k}<1
, then
\rho(t_{k})=T-t_{k}=r^{k}T
and
h_{k}/\rho(t_{k})=\frac{r^{k}h_{\max}}{r^{k}T}=\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\leq h_{\max}
since
T>1
.
Thus
h_{k}C_{t_{k}}\leq Ah_{\max}
for all
k
.
Choosing
h_{\max}\leq\bar{h}
small enough, we ensure
h_{k}C_{t_{k}}\leq 1
, hence
C_{t_{k}}\leq 1/h_{k}
.

Then for all
z,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\bigl\|\bigl(\text{Id}+h_{k}\nabla a_{t_{k}}(z)\bigr)v\bigr\|\leq\|\text{Id}+h_{k}\nabla a_{t_{k}}(z)\|_{\text{op}}\|v\|\leq(1+h_{k}L_{t_{k}})\|v\|\leq e^{h_{k}L_{t_{k}}}\|v\|,

as
\lambda_{\min}(\text{Id}+h_{k}\nabla a_{t_{k}}(z))\geq 0
. Hence,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\Delta_{k}+h_{k}\bigl(a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\bigr)\bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\bigl\|\bigl(\text{Id}+h_{k}\nabla a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}}+s(\bar{X}_{k}-X_{t_{k}})))\bigr)(X_{t_{k}}-\bar{X}_{k})\bigr\|ds

\displaystyle\leq e^{h_{k}L_{t_{k}}}\|\Delta_{k}\|.

(125)

Step 2: Itô formula for
a_{t}(X_{t})
and decomposition of
V_{k}
.

Define

\beta_{t}(x):=\Bigl(\partial_{t}a_{t}+(\nabla a_{t})a_{t}+\tfrac{1}{2}b_{t}^{2}\Delta a_{t}\Bigr)(x),\qquad G_{t}(x):=\nabla a_{t}(x).

By Itô’s formula applied to
a_{t}(X_{t})
,

d\bigl(a_{t}(X_{t})\bigr)=\beta_{t}(X_{t})dt+b_{t}G_{t}(X_{t})dW_{t}.

(126)

Writing
w_{k}(u):=t_{k+1}-u
, we obtain

\displaystyle V_{k}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\Big(a_{t}(X_{t})-a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})\Big)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)\beta_{u}(X_{u})du+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)b_{u}G_{u}(X_{u})dW_{u}.

(127)

Let
\mathcal{F}_{k}:=\sigma(X_{t_{k}},\bar{X}_{k})\subset\mathcal{F}_{t_{k}}
.
Since the stochastic integral in (
C.1.3
) is a martingale increment,
we have

\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)\beta_{u}(X_{u})du\;\middle|\;\mathcal{F}_{k}\right],

so by Minkowski,

\|\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)\|\beta_{u}(X_{u})\|_{L^{2}}du.

(128)

Moreover, by triangle inequality and Itô isometry,

\|V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)\|\beta_{u}(X_{u})\|_{L^{2}}du+\Bigl(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}w_{k}(u)^{2}b_{u}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|G_{u}(X_{u})\|_{F}^{2}du\Bigr)^{1/2}.

(129)

Step 3: apply the bias-variance accumulation lemma.

By the global Lipschitz bound
\|G_{u}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq C_{u}
and (
17
),

\mathbb{E}\|G_{u}(X_{u})\|_{F}^{2}\leq dC_{u}^{2}\leq d\frac{A^{2}}{\rho(u)^{2}}.

(130)

Next, using the bound based on Assumption
10
, the moment bound on
(X_{t})
, and the Laplacian bound,
we obtain

\|\beta_{u}(X_{u})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\rho(u)^{2}}\sqrt{d}.

(131)

Define

\text{Bias}_{k}:=C\sqrt{d}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{t_{k+1}-u}{\rho(u)^{2}}du,\qquad\text{Var}_{k}:=\text{Bias}_{k}+C\sqrt{d}\Bigl(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}}{\rho(u)^{2}}du\Bigr)^{1/2},

Then (
128
), (
129
), (
131
), (
130
) give

\|\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}\leq\text{Bias}_{k}
and

\|V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}\leq\text{Var}_{k}
.
Together with the stability (
C.1.3
), Proposition
17
yields

\|X_{\tau}-\bar{X}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda_{k}\text{Bias}_{k}+\Bigl(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda_{k}^{2}\text{Var}_{k}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2},\qquad\Lambda_{k}:=\exp\Bigl(\int_{t_{k+1}}^{\tau}L_{s}ds\Bigr).

(132)

Since
\sup_{z}\int_{z}^{\tau}L_{s}ds\leq A
, we have
\Lambda_{k}\leq e^{A}
for all
k
.

Step 4: geometric-grid estimates.

We use the pointwise bound

\frac{1}{\rho(u)^{2}}\leq 1+\frac{1}{(T-u)^{2}},\qquad u\in[0,\tau],

which follows from
\rho(u)=\min\{1,T-u\}
.
Therefore,

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{t_{k+1}-u}{\rho(u)^{2}}du\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}(t_{k+1}-u)du+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{t_{k+1}-u}{(T-u)^{2}}du=\frac{h_{k}^{2}}{2}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{t_{k+1}-u}{(T-u)^{2}}du.

For the geometric grid
(\tau,T,N)
, one has
T-t_{k}=r^{k}T
,
T-t_{k+1}=r^{k+1}T
, and
h_{k}=r^{k}h_{\max}
, and the same computation as in the original proof gives

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{t_{k+1}-u}{(T-u)^{2}}du=\log\frac{1}{r}+(r-1)=-\log\Bigl(1-\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\Bigr)-\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\leq C\Bigl(\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\Bigr)^{2},

(133)

for
h_{\max}\leq\bar{h}
small enough (in particular ensuring
h_{\max}/T\leq 1/2
).
Moreover, since
r^{N}=(T-\tau)/T
,

N=\frac{\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}}{\log\frac{1}{r}}=\frac{\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}}{-\log\bigl(1-\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\bigr)}\leq\frac{T}{h_{\max}}\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}.

(134)

Using
\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}=\tau
and
h_{k}\leq h_{\max}
,

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}^{2}\leq h_{\max}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}=h_{\max}\tau\leq h_{\max}T.

Therefore,

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\text{Bias}_{k}\leq C\sqrt{d}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}^{2}+N\Bigl(\frac{h_{\max}}{T}\Bigr)^{2}\right)\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\left(T+\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}\right),

(135)

where the last step uses (
134
) and
T>1
.

For the variance term, write
\text{Var}_{k}\leq\text{Bias}_{k}+S_{k}
where

S_{k}:=C\sqrt{d}\Bigl(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}}{\rho(u)^{2}}du\Bigr)^{1/2}.

Then

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\text{Var}_{k}^{2}\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\text{Bias}_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}S_{k}^{2}.

We again use
1/\rho(u)^{2}\leq 1+1/(T-u)^{2}
to obtain

S_{k}^{2}\leq Cd\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}du+Cd\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}}{(T-u)^{2}}du.

The first integral equals
h_{k}^{3}/3
, hence

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}^{3}\leq h_{\max}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}=h_{\max}^{2}\tau\leq h_{\max}^{2}T.

For the second integral, using
(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}\leq h_{k}^{2}
and
(T-u)\geq(T-t_{k+1})
for
u\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}]
,

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}}{(T-u)^{2}}du\leq\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{h_{k}^{2}}{(T-t_{k+1})^{2}}du=\frac{h_{k}^{3}}{(T-t_{k+1})^{2}}=\frac{(r^{k}h_{\max})^{3}}{(r^{k+1}T)^{2}}=r^{k-2}\frac{h_{\max}^{3}}{T^{2}}.

If
h_{\max}/T\leq 1/2
, then
r=1-h_{\max}/T\geq 1/2
and thus

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}r^{k-2}\leq C\frac{1}{1-r}=C\frac{T}{h_{\max}}.

Therefore,

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{(t_{k+1}-u)^{2}}{(T-u)^{2}}du\leq C\frac{h_{\max}^{3}}{T^{2}}\cdot\frac{T}{h_{\max}}=C\frac{h_{\max}^{2}}{T}.

Combining the two contributions yields

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}S_{k}^{2}\leq Cdh_{\max}^{2}\left(T+\frac{1}{T}\right),\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\Bigl(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}S_{k}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\sqrt{T}\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}T,

where we used
T>1
.
Also
\sqrt{\sum\text{Bias}_{k}^{2}}\leq\sum\text{Bias}_{k}
, so using (
135
) we obtain

\Bigl(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\text{Var}_{k}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\left(T+\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}\right).

(136)

Conclusion.

Plugging (
135
) and (
136
) into (
132
), using
\Lambda_{k}\leq e^{A}
, we obtain

\|X_{\tau}-\bar{X}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\left(T+\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}\right).

Combining with (
123
) yields the claim.
∎

C.1.4
Proof of Proposition
3

Proof.

We write the proof in the reduced model

X_{t}=f_{t}Y+\bar{g}_{t}\xi,\qquad Y\sim p^{\star},\ \xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})\ \text{independent},

where
\bar{g}_{t}=\sigma_{t}
in the Lipman setting and
\bar{g}_{t}=(g_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{t}^{2})^{1/2}
in the
Stochastic-interpolant setting. The two cases are identical once written in this form. We verify the hypotheses of Proposition
2
on
[0,\tau]
.

Step 1: Lipschitz bounds.
By Corollaries
1
/
5
/
8

in the Lipman case, and by Corollaries
2
/
6
/
9
in the
stochastic-interpolant case, there exists a dimension-free constant
C>0
such that for a.e.
t\in(0,\tau)
,

\int_{t}^{\tau}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\max}(\nabla v_{s}(x))\,ds\leq C,\qquad\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla v_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},

and

\|\partial_{t}v_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}\bigl(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\bigr),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Thus Assumption
10
-(A1),(A2),(A3) hold with

L_{t}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\max}(\nabla v_{t}(x)),\qquad C_{t}:=\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad M_{t}:=\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}}.

Step 2: linear-growth bound on
v_{t}
.

Set

\mu_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}[Y\mid X_{t}=x],\qquad a_{t}:=\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}},\qquad c_{t}:=f_{t}^{\prime}-a_{t}f_{t},\qquad D_{t}:=\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}.

Since

\dot{X}_{t}=f_{t}^{\prime}Y+\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}\xi=a_{t}X_{t}+c_{t}Y,

taking conditional expectation given
X_{t}=x
yields

v_{t}(x)=a_{t}x+c_{t}\mu_{t}(x).

By Lemma
16
,

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\left(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{f_{t}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}}\|x\|\right),\qquad\gamma_{t}:=\alpha+\frac{f_{t}^{2}}{\bar{g}_{t}^{2}},

hence, since
\bar{g}_{t}^{2}\gamma_{t}=D_{t}
,

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\left(\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\gamma_{t}}}+\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\|x\|\right).

Moreover Lemma
10
gives

\|\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{t,0}}[W]\|\leq\|\argmin u\|+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\alpha+f_{t}^{2}/\bar{g}_{t}^{2}}}\leq C\sqrt{d},

and also
\gamma_{t}\geq\alpha
, so
\sqrt{d/\gamma_{t}}\leq C\sqrt{d}
. Therefore,

\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{d}+\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\|x\|\right).

Consequently,

\|v_{t}(x)\|\leq|a_{t}|\,\|x\|+|c_{t}|\,\|\mu_{t}(x)\|\leq\left(|a_{t}|+C|c_{t}|\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\right)\|x\|+C|c_{t}|\sqrt{d}.

We now bound the coefficients. On
[t_{0},\tau]
, Assumption
9
gives

\bar{g}_{t}=(1-t)^{p}\ell(t),\qquad 0<A^{-1}\leq\ell(t)\leq A,\qquad\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq A,

hence

|a_{t}|=\left|\frac{\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}}{\bar{g}_{t}}\right|=\left|-\frac{p}{1-t}+\frac{\ell^{\prime}(t)}{\ell(t)}\right|\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad|c_{t}|\leq|f_{t}^{\prime}|+|a_{t}|\,|f_{t}|\leq\frac{C}{1-t}.

Also
f
is increasing and
f_{1}=1
, so
f_{t}\geq f_{t_{0}}>0
on
[t_{0},\tau]
, and therefore

D_{t}=\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}\geq f_{t_{0}}^{2}.

It follows that

|a_{t}|+|c_{t}|\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad|c_{t}|\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad t\in[t_{0},\tau].

On
(0,t_{0}]
, Assumption
9
gives

\bar{g}_{t}^{-1}+|\bar{g}_{t}^{\prime}|+|f_{t}^{\prime}|\leq C,

hence
|a_{t}|+|c_{t}|\leq C
. Since
0\leq f_{t}\leq 1
and
\bar{g}_{t}\geq C^{-1}
on
(0,t_{0}]
,

D_{t}=\alpha\bar{g}_{t}^{2}+f_{t}^{2}\geq c_{0}>0,

so

|a_{t}|+|c_{t}|\frac{f_{t}}{D_{t}}\leq C,\qquad|c_{t}|\leq C,\qquad t\in(0,t_{0}].

Combining the two regimes, we obtain for all
t\in(0,\tau]
,

\|v_{t}(x)\|\leq\frac{C}{1-t}\bigl(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|\bigr).

Thus Assumption
10
-(A4) holds with

F_{t}:=\frac{C}{1-t}.

Step 3: moment bound for the Euler scheme.

Let
\hat{X}_{t}
be the continuous Euler interpolation:
for
t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1})
,

\dot{\hat{X}}_{t}=v_{t_{k}}(\hat{X}_{t}),\qquad\hat{X}_{t_{k}}=\bar{X}_{k}.

Then
\hat{X}_{t_{k}}=\bar{X}_{k}
for all
k
. First, by Proposition
6
,

\|Y\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d},

while
\|\xi\|_{L_{2}}=\sqrt{d}
and
f_{t},\bar{g}_{t}
are bounded on
[0,\tau]
, so

\|X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\leq|f_{t}|\,\|Y\|_{L_{2}}+|\bar{g}_{t}|\,\|\xi\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}.

Now define

\Delta_{t}:=\hat{X}_{t}-X_{t},\qquad\delta_{t}:=\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L_{2}},\qquad\underline{t}:=t_{k}\ \text{for }t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}).

For a.e.
t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1})
,

\dot{\Delta}_{t}=v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{t}(X_{t})=\bigl(v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{t}(X_{t})\bigr)+\bigl(v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})\bigr).

Hence

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}=\langle\Delta_{t},v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{t}(X_{t})\rangle+\langle\Delta_{t},v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})\rangle\leq L_{t}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}+\|\Delta_{t}\|\,\|v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})\|.

Therefore, for a.e.
t
such that
\delta_{t}>0
,

\delta_{t}^{\prime}\leq L_{t}\delta_{t}+\|v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})\|_{L_{2}}.

Using the time-Lipschitz bound at the fixed space point
\hat{X}_{t}
,

\|v_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})-v_{\underline{t}}(\hat{X}_{t})\|_{L_{2}}\leq\int_{\underline{t}}^{t}\|\partial_{s}v_{s}(\hat{X}_{t})\|_{L_{2}}\,ds\leq\left(\int_{\underline{t}}^{t}M_{s}\,ds\right)\bigl(\sqrt{d}+\|\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\bigr).

Since
\|\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\leq\|X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}+\delta_{t}\leq C\sqrt{d}+\delta_{t}
, we get

\delta_{t}^{\prime}\leq\bigl(L_{t}+\eta_{t}\bigr)\delta_{t}+C\sqrt{d}\,\eta_{t},\qquad\eta_{t}:=\int_{\underline{t}}^{t}M_{s}\,ds.

By Grönwall’s lemma and
\delta_{0}=0
,

\delta_{t}\leq C\sqrt{d}\int_{0}^{t}\eta_{s}\exp\!\left(\int_{s}^{t}(L_{u}+\eta_{u})\,du\right)ds.

Now it remains to bound
\int_{0}^{\tau}\eta_{s}\,ds
. On each interval
[t_{k},t_{k+1})
,

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\eta_{s}\,ds=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\int_{t_{k}}^{s}M_{u}\,du\,ds=\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}(t_{k+1}-u)M_{u}\,du\leq h_{k}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{C\,du}{(1-u)^{2}}\leq C\frac{h_{k}^{2}}{(1-t_{k})^{2}}.

Since
h_{k}=(1-r)(1-t_{k})=h_{\max}(1-t_{k})
on the geometric grid,

\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\eta_{s}\,ds\leq Ch_{\max}^{2}.

Summing over
k
yields

\int_{0}^{\tau}\eta_{s}\,ds\leq CNh_{\max}^{2}.

Moreover, if

x:=\frac{1}{N}\log\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\right),\qquad r=e^{-x},\qquad h_{\max}=1-r,

then
h_{\max}\leq x
, hence

Nh_{\max}^{2}\leq h_{\max}\log\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\right).

Therefore

\int_{0}^{\tau}\eta_{s}\,ds\leq Ch_{\max}\log\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\right).

If
h_{\max}\leq\bar{h}\log\!\bigl(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\bigr)^{-1}
with
\bar{h}
small enough, then

\int_{0}^{\tau}\eta_{s}\,ds\leq C,

and the previous Grönwall bound gives

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|\hat{X}_{t}-X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,h_{\max}\log\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\right)\leq C\sqrt{d}.

In particular, at the grid points,

\sup_{0\leq k\leq N}\|\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L_{2}}=\sup_{0\leq k\leq N}\|\hat{X}_{t_{k}}\|_{L_{2}}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|\hat{X}_{t}-X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}.

Conclusion.

We have verified all hypotheses of Proposition
2
with

\sup_{z\in[0,\tau]}\int_{z}^{\tau}L_{t}\,dt\leq C,\qquad F_{t}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad C_{t}\leq\frac{C}{1-t},\qquad M_{t}\leq\frac{C}{(1-t)^{2}},\qquad\sup_{0\leq k\leq N}\|\bar{X}_{k}\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}.

Therefore, Proposition
2
applies and yields

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}\,h_{\max}\log\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\right).

∎

C.1.5
Proof of Proposition
17

Proof.

We work on a probability space carrying the same
d
-dimensional Brownian motion
(W_{t})_{t\in[0,\tau]}

and the same initial condition
X_{0}=\bar{X}_{0}
.
By definition of the
2
-Wasserstein distance,

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq\|X_{\tau}-\bar{X}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}=\|\Delta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}.

Subtracting the Euler step and using the same Brownian increment yields

\displaystyle\Delta_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}a_{t}(X_{t})dt-h_{k}a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{k}+h_{k}\big(a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big)+V_{k},

(137)

where
V_{k}
is defined in (
120
). Defining,

A_{k}:=\Delta_{k}+h_{k}\big(a_{t_{k}}(X_{t_{k}})-a_{t_{k}}(\bar{X}_{k})\big),\qquad B_{k}:=\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}],\qquad M_{k+1}:=V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}].

we have

\Delta_{k+1}=A_{k}+B_{k}+M_{k+1}.

(138)

Since
A_{k}+B_{k}
is
\mathcal{F}_{k}
-measurable and
\mathbb{E}[M_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\langle A_{k}+B_{k},M_{k+1}\rangle\big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\langle A_{k}+B_{k},M_{k+1}\rangle\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\big]\Big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\langle A_{k}+B_{k},\mathbb{E}[M_{k+1}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\rangle\big]=0.

Therefore,

\|\Delta_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|A_{k}+B_{k}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|M_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|A_{k}+\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(139)

By assumptions (
121
)–(
122
), we have

\displaystyle\|A_{k}+\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}
