\displaystyle\leq\|A_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}L_{s}ds}\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\mathrm{Bias}_{k},

\displaystyle\|V_{k}-\mathbb{E}[V_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}]\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Var}_{k},

so
plugging into (
139
) gives

\|\Delta_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\Big(\Lambda(t_{k},t_{k+1})\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\mathrm{Bias}_{k}\Big)^{2}+\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2},\qquad\Lambda(s,t):=\exp\Big(\int_{s}^{t}L_{u}du\Big).

(140)

For convenience set
\alpha_{k}:=\Lambda(t_{k},t_{k+1})>0
.
Define two nonnegative sequences
(S_{k})_{k=0}^{N}
and
(T_{k})_{k=0}^{N}
by

S_{0}:=0,\qquad T_{0}:=0,\qquad S_{k+1}:=\alpha_{k}S_{k}+\mathrm{Bias}_{k},\qquad T_{k+1}:=\alpha_{k}^{2}T_{k}+\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}.

We claim that for all
k\in\{0,\dots,N\}
,

\|\Delta_{k}\|_{L^{2}}\leq S_{k}+\sqrt{T_{k}}.

(141)

This is true for
k=0
since
\|X_{0}-\bar{X}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}=0
.
Assume (
141
) holds at step
k
.
Using (
140
),

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\big(\alpha_{k}(S_{k}+\sqrt{T_{k}})+\mathrm{Bias}_{k}\big)^{2}+\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{k}S_{k}+\mathrm{Bias}_{k}+\sqrt{(\alpha_{k}\sqrt{T_{k}})^{2}+\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq S_{k+1}+\sqrt{T_{k+1}},

which proves (
141
) by induction. Now, by iterating the recursions for
S_{k}
and
T_{k}
, we obtain

S_{N}=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Big(\prod_{j=k+1}^{N-1}\alpha_{j}\Big)\mathrm{Bias}_{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda(t_{k+1},\tau)\mathrm{Bias}_{k},

and

T_{N}=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Big(\prod_{j=k+1}^{N-1}\alpha_{j}\Big)^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda(t_{k+1},\tau)^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}.

Combining with (
141
) at
k=N
yields

\|\Delta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\leq S_{N}+\sqrt{T_{N}}=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda(t_{k+1},\tau)\mathrm{Bias}_{k}+\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\Lambda(t_{k+1},\tau)^{2}\mathrm{Var}_{k}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

Together with
W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N}))\leq\|\Delta_{N}\|_{L^{2}}
, this gives the result.
∎

C.1.6
Proof of Proposition
4

Proof.

We apply Theorem
5
with diffusion coefficient

b_{t}\equiv\sqrt{2}
and drift

a_{t}(x)=x+2s_{t}(x),\qquad s_{t}(x):=\nabla\log p_{t}(x),\qquad p_{t}:=q_{T-t},\quad t\in[0,T].

Recall
\rho(t):=\min\{1,T-t\}
and set

\theta(t):=e^{-(T-t)}\in(0,1).

We will repeatedly use the elementary comparability (for
u\geq 0
)

1-e^{-u}\gtrsim\min\{1,u\},\qquad 1-e^{-2u}\gtrsim\min\{1,u\}.

(142)

First,
Since
s_{t}=\nabla\log p_{t}
is a gradient field,
\nabla a_{t}

is symmetric. For the moment bound, we use that the reverse SDE in Proposition
4
is the time-reversal
of the OU path, so
\text{Law}(X_{t})=p_{t}
for
t\in[0,\tau]
. Then,

\mathbb{E}\|X_{t}\|^{2}=\theta(t)^{2}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+(1-\theta(t)^{2})\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+d.

Using Proposition
6
we have
\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}\lesssim d
so

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{d}.

Let us now verify the assumptions (A1)-(A4)

(A1)

Because
\nabla a_{t}
is symmetric,

L_{t}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\max}(\nabla a_{t}(x))=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lambda_{\max}(I_{d}+2\nabla s_{t}(x)),

so

\sup_{z\in[0,\tau]}\int_{z}^{\tau}L_{s}ds=\sup_{z\in[0,\tau]}\int_{\theta(z)}^{\theta(\tau)}\frac{1}{\theta}\sup_{x}\lambda_{\max}\big(I_{d}+2\nabla s_{\theta}(x)\big)d\theta\leq 2\int_{\theta(z)}^{\theta(\tau)}\frac{1}{\theta}\sup_{x}\lambda_{\max}\big(I_{d}+\nabla s_{\theta}(x)\big)d\theta.

By Corollary
3
, the last integral is bounded by a constant
depending only on
(\alpha,\beta,K)
, uniformly over
z
, hence

\sup_{z\in[0,\tau]}\int_{z}^{\tau}L_{s}ds\leq C
.

(A2).

By Corollary
7
, we have

C_{t}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\nabla a_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}=\sup_{x}\|I_{d}+2\nabla s_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\lesssim\frac{1}{1-\theta(t)^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\rho(t)}.

(A3).

By Corollary
10
, we have

\|\partial_{t}a_{t}(x)\|=2\|\partial_{t}s_{t}(x)\|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{d}+\|x\|}{\rho(t)^{2}},

(A4)

By Lemma
9
, we get

\|a_{t}(x)\|\leq\|x\|+2\|s_{t}(x)\|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}+\|x\|}{\rho(t)}.

Let us now give a bound on
\|\Delta a_{t}\|
. The Fokker-Planck equation gives

\partial_{t}p_{t}=-\nabla\cdot(xp_{t})-\Delta p_{t}.

(143)

Let
g_{t}:=\log p_{t}
, so
s_{t}=\nabla g_{t}
. Dividing (
143
) by
p_{t}
and using

\Delta p_{t}/p_{t}=\Delta g_{t}+\|\nabla g_{t}\|^{2}
and
\nabla\cdot(xp_{t})/p_{t}=d+x\cdot\nabla g_{t}
gives

\partial_{t}g_{t}=-(d+x\cdot\nabla g_{t})-\Delta g_{t}-\|\nabla g_{t}\|^{2}.

Taking a gradient yields the score PDE

\partial_{t}s_{t}+s_{t}+(\nabla s_{t})(x+2s_{t})+\Delta s_{t}=0,

(144)

so

\|\Delta a_{t}(x)\|\leq 2\|\partial_{t}s_{t}(x)\|+2\|s_{t}(x)\|+2\|\nabla s_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\|x+2s_{t}(x)\|.

Using the bounds established above:

\|\partial_{t}s_{t}(x)\|\lesssim\rho(t)^{-2}(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|),\qquad\|s_{t}(x)\|\lesssim\rho(t)^{-1}(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|),\qquad\|\nabla s_{t}(x)\|_{\text{op}}\lesssim\rho(t)^{-1},

and
\|x+2s_{t}(x)\|=\|a_{t}(x)\|\lesssim\rho(t)^{-1}(\sqrt{d}+\|x\|)
, we obtain

\|\Delta a_{t}(x)\|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{d}+\|x\|}{\rho(t)^{2}},

which is exactly the Laplacian hypothesis of Theorem
5
.

All assumptions of Theorem
5
are satisfied
so for
h_{\max}
small enough we have

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\Big(T+\log\Big(\frac{T}{T-\tau}\Big)\Big).

Finally, using
T\leq A\log((T-\tau)^{-1})
and
\log T\leq T
, we get

T+\log\Big(\frac{T}{T-\tau}\Big)=T+\log T+\log\Big(\frac{1}{T-\tau}\Big)\leq(2A+1)\log\Big(\frac{1}{T-\tau}\Big).

which gives the result.
∎

C.2
Proofs of the sampling errors

C.2.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Proof.

First, we have

W_{2}\bigl(p^{\star},\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq W_{2}\bigl(p^{\star},\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\bigr)+W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigl)

and
p^{\star}=\text{Law}(X_{T})
.
Now, let
\hat{X}
be the (learned) solution of

d\hat{X}_{t}=\hat{a}_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})dt+b_{t}dW_{t},\qquad t\in[0,\tau],

with the same initial condition
X_{0}=\hat{X}_{0}
in law. We decompose

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau})\bigr)+W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr).

(145)

Couple
X
and
\hat{X}
by taking the same Brownian motion
W
and the same initial condition. Let

\Delta_{t}:=X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t},

we have

W_{2}^{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau})\bigr)\leq\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|^{2}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\bigl\langle\Delta_{t},a_{t}(X_{t})-\hat{a}_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})\bigr\rangle

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\bigl\langle\Delta_{t},\hat{a}_{t}(X_{t})-\hat{a}_{t}(\hat{X}_{t})\bigr\rangle+2\mathbb{E}\bigl\langle\Delta_{t},a_{t}(X_{t})-\hat{a}_{t}(X_{t})\bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2\hat{L}_{t}\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{t}\|^{2}+2\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\epsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t)

using the one-sided Lipschitzness of
\hat{a}_{t}

and Cauchy-Schwarz.
Finally, applying the non-linear Grönwall’s inequality we get

\left(\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\exp\Bigl(\int_{t}^{\tau}\hat{L}_{s}ds\Bigr)\epsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t)dt.

∎

C.2.2
Proof of Corollary
11

Proof.

We only give the detail of the proof in the Lipman setting as the proof is the same in the Stochastic interpolant setting by replacing Corollaries
5
and
8
by Corollaries
6
and
9
.
Applying Lemma
1
, we have
for
(\hat{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,\tau]}

\displaystyle W_{2}\bigl(p^{\star},\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq

\displaystyle W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{1}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\bigr)+\int_{0}^{\tau}\exp\Bigl(\int_{t}^{\tau}\hat{L}_{s}ds\Bigr)\epsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t)dt+W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr).

for
\hat{X}_{t}
the solution of

\partial_{t}\hat{X}_{t}=\hat{v}_{t}(\hat{X}_{t}),\qquad\hat{X}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}X_{0},

Early stopping error
W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{1}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau}))
.

In the Lipman reduced-model setting, the marginal at time
t
can be represented as

X_{t}\sim f_{t}Y+\sigma_{t}\xi,\qquad Y\sim p^{\star},\ \xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d}),\ Y\perp\xi,

so in particular
X_{1}\sim Y\sim p^{\star}
and
X_{\tau}\sim f_{\tau}Y+\sigma_{\tau}\xi
.
Applying Lemma
11
with
A=f_{\tau}\text{Id}
and
\eta=\sigma_{\tau}\xi
yields

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{1}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\bigr)\leq\Bigl((1-f_{\tau})^{2}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|\sigma_{\tau}\xi\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}=\Bigl((1-f_{\tau})^{2}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+d\sigma_{\tau}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}.

(146)

Under the assumptions
\|\argmin u\|\leq A\sqrt{d}
, one has
\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}\leq Cd
by Proposition
6
,
so (
146
) gives

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{1}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}\bigl(|1-f_{\tau}|+\sigma_{\tau}\bigr).

(147)

Next, since
f^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(0,1)
and
f_{1}=1
, we have

|1-f_{\tau}|=\Bigl|\int_{\tau}^{1}f^{\prime}(s)ds\Bigr|\leq\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}(1-\tau).

By the schedule assumption,
\sigma_{\tau}\leq A(1-\tau)^{p}
for
\tau
close enough to
1
.
Set

q:=p\wedge 1,\qquad\tau=1-\Bigl(\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}\Bigr)^{1/q}.

Writing

x_{N}:=\frac{\log^{2}N}{N},

we have
1-\tau=x_{N}^{1/q}
. Hence

\sigma_{\tau}\leq C(1-\tau)^{p}=Cx_{N}^{p/q}\leq Cx_{N}=C\frac{\log^{2}N}{N},

since
p/q\geq 1
, and

|1-f_{\tau}|\leq C(1-\tau)=Cx_{N}^{1/q}\leq Cx_{N}=C\frac{\log^{2}N}{N},

since
1/q\geq 1
.
Plugging into (
147
) yields

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(X_{1}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}.

(148)

Discretization error
W_{2}(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N}))
.

By the same arguments than in the proof of Proposition
3
,
\hat{v}_{t}
satisfies the hypotheses (
16
) of Proposition
2
. It remains to verify the moment assumption in Proposition
2
for the learned dynamics. Let
(\hat{X}_{t})_{t\in[0,\tau]}
solve

\dot{\hat{X}}_{t}=\hat{v}_{t}(\hat{X}_{t}),\qquad\hat{X}_{0}=X_{0}.

Coupling
(X_{t},\hat{X}_{t})
with the same initial condition and setting
\Delta_{t}:=X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}
, the same computation as in Lemma
1
yields

\frac{d}{dt}\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\hat{L}_{t}\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t),

hence, by Grönwall and the integrated one-sided Lipschitz bound on
\hat{v}
,

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\int_{0}^{\tau}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(s)\,ds\leq C\sqrt{d}.

On the other hand, by Proposition
6
and the explicit representation of the exact interpolation, we have

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}.

Therefore,

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\|X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d},

so that

\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{X}_{t}\|^{2}\leq Cd.

We may now repeat verbatim Step 3 in the proof of Proposition
3
with
\hat{X}_{t}
in place of
X_{t}
, which gives

\sup_{k=0,\dots,N}\mathbb{E}\|\bar{X}_{k}\|^{2}\leq Cd,

and thus the hypotheses of Proposition
2
are satisfied for the learned drift
\hat{v}
. Therefore, Proposition
2

yields for
h_{\max}
small enough,

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Bigr).

(149)

We now relate
h_{\max}
and
N
for the geometric grid:
since
r^{N}=1-\tau
and
h_{\max}=1-r
, we can write

r=\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{N}\log\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Bigr)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad h_{\max}=1-e^{-x}\leq x\quad\text{with}\quad x:=\frac{1}{N}\log\frac{1}{1-\tau}.

Therefore,

h_{\max}\leq\frac{1}{N}\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Bigr).

(150)

Combining (
149
) and (
150
) gives

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}\frac{\log^{2}\bigl(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\bigr)}{N}.

(151)

With
\tau=1-(\log^{2}N/N)^{1/q}
we have

\log\Bigl(\frac{1}{1-\tau}\Bigr)=\frac{1}{q}\log\Bigl(\frac{N}{\log^{2}N}\Bigr)\leq\frac{1}{q}\log N\qquad\text{for $N$ large enough.}

hence (
151
) implies

W_{2}\bigl(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\bigr)\leq C\sqrt{d}\frac{\log^{2}N}{N}.

(152)

∎

C.2.3
Proof of Corollary
12

Proof.

We decompose,

W_{2}\big(p^{\star},\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{T}),\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\big)+W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau})\big)+W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)

and bound separately each term.

1- Early stopping.

Since
\text{Law}(X_{T})=p^{\star}
and
\text{Law}(X_{\tau})=q_{T-\tau}=q_{1/N^{2}}
, and

q_{s}=\text{Law}(e^{-s}Y+\sqrt{1-e^{-2s}}\xi)
with
Y\sim p^{\star}
and
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
independent, we may couple

Y
with
e^{-s}Y+\sqrt{1-e^{-2s}}\xi
and obtain

W_{2}\big(p^{\star},q_{s}\big)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big\|Y-\Big(e^{-s}Y+\sqrt{1-e^{-2s}}\xi\Big)\Big\|^{2}=(1-e^{-s})^{2}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+(1-e^{-2s})\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|^{2}.

With
s=T-\tau=1/N^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|^{2}=d
, using
1-e^{-s}\leq s
and
1-e^{-2s}\leq 2s
gives

W_{2}\big(p^{\star},\text{Law}(X_{\tau})\big)=W_{2}\big(p^{\star},q_{1/N^{2}}\big)\leq\Big(\frac{\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}}{N^{4}}+\frac{2d}{N^{2}}\Big)^{1/2}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{N},

by Proposition
6
.

2- Learning error.

Couple
(X_{0},\hat{X}_{0})
optimally so that
\|X_{0}-\hat{X}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}=W_{2}(q_{T},\gamma)
, and drive
(X_{t},\hat{X}_{t})

with the same Brownian motion. With
\Delta_{t}=X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}
, the same computation as above yields

\frac{d}{dt}\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\hat{L}_{t}\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t),

hence by Grönwall,

W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}))\leq\|\Delta_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{\int_{0}^{\tau}\hat{L}_{s}ds}W_{2}(q_{T},\gamma)+\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{\int_{t}^{\tau}\hat{L}_{s}ds}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t)dt.

Using the bound
e^{\sup_{z\in[0,\tau]}\int_{z}^{\tau}\hat{L}_{s}ds}\leq C
, it remains to control
W_{2}(q_{T},\gamma)
.
Couple
q_{T}
with
\gamma_{d}
by pairing
Z_{T}
with the same
\xi
. Then

W_{2}(q_{T},\gamma)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\|Z_{T}-\xi\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big\|e^{-T}Y+\big(\sqrt{1-e^{-2T}}-1\big)\xi\Big\|^{2}.

Using
\big(1-\sqrt{1-u}\big)=\frac{u}{1+\sqrt{1-u}}\leq u
for
u\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{E}\|Z_{T}-\xi\|^{2}\leq 2e^{-2T}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+2\big(1-\sqrt{1-e^{-2T}}\big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|^{2}\leq 2e^{-2T}\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}+2e^{-4T}d.

Hence

W_{2}(q_{T},\gamma)\leq\sqrt{2}e^{-T}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|Y\|^{2}}+\sqrt{2d}e^{-2T}\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{N}.

Therefore,

W_{2}(\text{Law}(X_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}))\leq C\frac{\sqrt{d}}{N}+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(t)dt.

3- Time discretization.

Set
\Delta_{t}:=X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}
. By Grönwall’s lemma,
for every
t\in[0,\tau]
,

\|\Delta_{t}\|_{L_{2}}\leq e^{\int_{0}^{t}\hat{L}_{s}ds}\|\Delta_{0}\|_{L_{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\int_{u}^{t}\hat{L}_{s}ds}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(u)du.

Since
\|\Delta_{0}\|_{L_{2}}\leq\|X_{0}\|_{L_{2}}+\|\hat{X}_{0}\|_{L_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d}
, this gives

\displaystyle\|\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|X_{t}\|_{L_{2}}+\|X_{t}-\hat{X}_{t}\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}+C\sqrt{d}+C\int_{0}^{\tau}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{drift}}(u)du

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}.

Then, by repeating verbatim the verification of the hypotheses of Theorem
5
carried out in the proof of Proposition
4
with
a_{t}
replaced by
\hat{a}_{t}=x+2\hat{s}_{t}
and using the assumed structural bounds on
\hat{s}_{t}
, we may apply Theorem
5
to
(\hat{X}_{t})_{t\leq\tau}
and its Euler scheme to obtain

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq C\sqrt{d}h_{\max}\log\Big(\frac{1}{T-\tau}\Big).

Since
r=((T-\tau)/T)^{1/N}=\exp\big(-\frac{1}{N}\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}\big)
, letting

x:=\frac{1}{N}\log\frac{T}{T-\tau}\geq 0
gives
r=e^{-x}
and

h_{\max}=T(1-r)=T(1-e^{-x})\leq Tx=\frac{T}{N}\log\Big(\frac{T}{T-\tau}\Big).

With
T=\log N
and
T-\tau=1/N^{2}
, this yields

h_{\max}\log\Big(\frac{1}{T-\tau}\Big)\leq\frac{\log N}{N}\log\big((\log N)N^{2}\big)\log(N^{2})\;\lesssim\;\frac{\log^{3}N}{N},

for
N
large enough. Therefore

W_{2}\big(\text{Law}(\hat{X}_{\tau}),\text{Law}(\bar{X}_{N})\big)\leq C\sqrt{d}\frac{\log^{3}N}{N}.

∎
